L268: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L269: Ophthalmoscopy	
Examinations were performed at pre-test, end of main study (week 12) and end of recovery (week 5).
5 mg/kg: glassy appearance of the fundus in both eyes (3M), conjunctiva appeared reddened and swollen (2M); findings were reversible
Fucithalmic eye drops for reddened eyes and/or reddened conjunctiva/swelling were given to animals (4M, 3F) for several days (multiple times for most animals) during the treatment period.
HuMax-TF, 25 mg/kg: glassy appearance of the fundus in both eyes (5M); findings were reversible

ECG	Unremarkable
Behavioral Assessments	Unremarkable
Respiratory Rates	Unremarkable
Hematology	
Hematology parameters were evaluated at pre-test, during dosing (pre-dose, 3-, 5-, 9-, 13- and 17-days post dose), and weekly during the recovery period. Coagulation parameters were evaluated at pre-test, during dosing (pre-dose, 1- and 13-days post dose), and week 1, 3, and 6 of the recovery period.
Statistically significant decrease in erythropoiesis was observed at 5 mg/kg beginning day 10 to 14 and lasted for the duration of the study; parameters were generally lower compared to control and baseline levels. Mean changes included up to -23% in red blood cell, up to -20% in hemoglobin, and up to -23% in hematocrit values compared to control.
Variable changes in reticulocytes were noted with generally decreased values in the range of approximately -50% to -90% up to 5 days post dose compared to control, followed by increased values prior to the subsequent dose at 5 mg/kg. A generally similar trend to a lesser magnitude was observed at 3 mg/kg.
A decrease in leukocytes (generally in the range of -35 to -50%) were observed up to about 9 to 10 days post dosing often reaching statistical significance at all doses in males and mostly at 5 mg/kg in females.
Generally, 3 days post dosing, an approximately 1.5-fold and 1.5 to 2-fold decrease in lymphocytes compared to baseline was observed at 3 and 5 mg/kg, respectively, with recovery prior to the next dose. The decrease was seen after the first two doses at the mid dose and after all doses at the high dose.
Approximately 9 days post dosing up to 6-fold and up to 18-fold decrease in neutrophils compared to baseline was observed at 3 and 5 mg/kg, respectively, with recovery trends prior to the next dose.
No drug-related changes in coagulation parameters were noted for all dose levels of tisotumab vedotin, but a 10 to 20% increase in prothrombin time was noted in the 25 mg/kg unconjugated tisotumab group.
Animals were treated with antibiotics (Baytril) due to marked neutropenia:
3 mg/kg: 20% of the animals after the first dose (week 2).
5 mg/kg: 100% of animals received antibiotics as a precaution following the first dose (Day 5 to 14) in anticipation of neutropenia; 70% of the animals received antibiotics due to marked neutropenia after the second and/or third dose.
L270: ## Changes in hematology parameters during the dosing period
L271: [Excerpted from the original IND (b) (4) review]
L272: The graphs where only male animals are shown are representative of results from both sexes.
L273: Clinical Chemistry	
Clinical chemistry parameters were evaluated at pre-test, during dosing (pre-dose, 1- and 13-days post dose), and week 1, 3, and 6 of the recovery period.
Changes in albumin, globulin, haptoglobin, and iron were observed at 5 mg/kg. Changes in other parameters were noted throughout the dosing period but were transient, within pretrial values, and/or independent of dose.
Decrease in albumin up to -20% and an increase in globulin up to 28% led to overall decrease in the albumin/globulin ratio up to -38% from Day 14 until the end of dosing period in both genders. Changes were most pronounced after the first dose.
Transient increase in haptoglobin up to 160% in both sexes throughout the dosing period with the most pronounced change after the first dose.
Decrease in iron (males only) up to -48%, however, not reaching statistical significance at every timepoint examined.

Urinalysis	Unremarkable

L274: Organ Weights	
None of the changes in organ weights attained statistical significance, but macroscopic findings in the lung (dark discoloration, adhesion, failure to collapse), microscopic findings in lymphoid tissues (decreased granulopoiesis and cellularity in bone marrow, decreased germinal centers in spleen) and macroscopic and microscopic findings in male reproductive organs (abnormal consistency, tubular atrophy in testes, and decreased or absence of sperm in epididymis) suggest toxicological relevance.

Microscopic findings are summarized in the table below. Similar findings were also observed at the end of study necropsy (Day 12) in 4 mg/kg interim study animals. L290: ## General toxicology; additional studies
L291: The main findings in the 13-week GLP toxicology study were reflective of toxicities seen with early non-GLP toxicology assessments with tisotumab vedotin. A noteworthy observation in the 10-week dose range finding study (Study# (b) (4) 521988) involved one animal given the high dose of 6 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin that experienced respiratory symptoms ten days after the first dose described as gasping, wheezing, shallow, and labored breathing that led to early euthanasia the same day. Necropsy revealed discolored spongy lungs with microscopic findings of mild edematous congestion/hemorrhage. In Study# (b) (4) 514850 bleeding time was assessed, the main findings are summarized below.
L292: ## **Study/ID: HuMab-TF (IgG1-1015-011): Pilot Repeat Dose Toxicology Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys**(b)(4) **514850 (non-GLP)**
L293: **Dosing:** In a non-GLP, intra-animal dose escalation study, cynomolgus monkeys (n=2/sex) were given an intravenous infusion over a 30-minute time period (10 mL/kg) of phosphate buffered saline on Day 1 and unconjugated HuMax-TF at 1, 10, and 100 mg/kg on Days 8, 15, and 22, respectively. The animals were observed for an additional 5 days after the last dosing and sacrificed on Day 27. Bleeding time was assessed in the study at 1, 24, and 120 hours post dose. A Simplet Bleeding Time Device was used to make an incision on the muscular area of the forearm, and the flow of blood was evaluated using a filter paper every 30 seconds until blood did not stain the filter paper twice. A TEG analyzer was used to assess the mechanical properties of the developing clot (clot formation, kinetics, strength, stability and resolution).
L294: **Results:** Bleeding time increased in a dose dependent manner by 13%, 65% and 93% at 1, 10, and 100 mg/kg, respectively, 1 hour after the infusion compared to the saline dosed Day 1 measurement. Statistical significance (p=0.012) was reached at 100 mg/kg. The mean serum concentration at 1, 10, and 100 mg/kg 1-hour post infusion was 23.5, 213, and 2255 µg/mL, respectively. Microscopic findings of up to moderate focal myocardial degeneration (females) with some inflammation and minimal hemorrhage in the heart (one female), perivascular cuffing of the brain (males), dark appearance of the urinary bladder with microscopic findings of hemorrhage (one female) and up to moderate pylorus gastritis in the stomach (one male, females) was noted at the end of the study. The Applicant did not attribute the findings as drug-related but rather as spontaneous background findings in the cynomolgus monkey; however, the study did not include a vehicle dosed control group for comparison.
L295: Additional GLP compliant general toxicology studies with MMAE in rat and monkey were previously reviewed by the FDA under the Applicant's BLA 125388 (brentuximab vedotin). The major findings based on the primary nonclinical review are summarized below for the MMAE dosed animals.
L296: ## **Study/ID: A 4-week intravenous injection toxicity and toxicokinetic study of SGN-35, SGD-1010, and SGN-30 in rats with a 4-week recovery/7646-118**
L297: **Dosing:** Rats were administered 0, 0.0097, 0.097, 0.194 mg/kg SGD-1010 (MMAE, lot# SGD-1010-0-09, 97.35% purity) once weekly via intravenous injection for 4 weeks.
L298: **Results:** No toxicities were observed at ≤0.097 mg/kg SGD-1010. The following were noted:
L299: - One animal at the mid dose died on Day 26 after blood collection and was evaluated as accidental.
- A -60% decrease in body weight was observed in animals given the high dose during Week 1. Body weight gradually increased and was within the range of control animals by Week 3.
- A decrease in erythropoiesis was reported at the high dose:Decrease in red blood cells (-43%), hemoglobin (-36%), and hematocrit (-38%) was noted. Reticulocytes were decreased at the mid and high dose by -49% and -91%, respectively.
L300: - An overall decrease of \ge 40\% was noted for leukocytes, neutrophil, lymphocytes, monocytes, and eosinophils in animals given the high dose.
- Elevated levels of total bilirubin, AST, ALT, ALP, and GGT were reported in animals given the high dose with microscopic correlates of necrosis in 3/10 males, suggesting liver damage.
- The major target organs were the bone marrow, testes/epididymides, and thymus generally in animals given the high dose.Bone marrow: mild to severe hypocellularity
- Testes: tubule degeneration and spermatocytes
- Thymus: mild to severe lymphocyte depletion and mild necrosis, correlating with an overall decrease in thymus weight
- All findings were reversible except in the testes and epididymides.
L301: **Study/ID:** Study/ID: An 11-week repeat-dose intravenous infusion toxicity study of SGN-35 and SGD-1010 in cynomolgus monkeys with a five-week recovery period/ (b) (4) 163.16
L302: **Dosing:** Monkeys were dosed with MMAE at 0.058 mg/kg (MMAE, lot# SGD-1010-0-09, 97.35% purity) once every three weeks via intravenous infusion for four total doses.
L303: **Results:** No changes in the body weight, blood pressure, heart rate, respiration, body temperature, ophthalmology, electrocardiogram, coagulation and urinalysis. The following changes were observed:
L304: - Decreased red blood cells (-10%), reticulocytes (-85%), white blood cells (-50%), monocytes (-50%), neutrophils (-65%), lymphocytes (-45%) and eosinophils (-85%) was observed at the end of treatment.
- Histopathology findings included:Hypocellularity, hemorrhage, congestion, and necrosis in hematopoietic tissues and lymphoid organs (thymus, spleen)
- All findings were reversible.
L305: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L306: The toxicity profile of tisotumab vedotin was established in a comprehensive series of nonclinical safety studies in cynomolgus monkeys, the only cross-reactive toxicology species.
L307: Target-mediated uptake of tisotumab vedotin led to dose limiting skin toxicity at approximately 4-fold the human systemic exposure (AUC) at the clinically recommended dose. Skin toxicity, in addition to conjunctival changes, correlated with TF expression. Bone marrow hypocellularity and testicular toxicity were the primary target-independent toxicities and were observed with both tisotumab vedotin and MMAE. Additional target organs identified following MMAE treatment included lymphoid organs, gastrointestinal tract, and liver. The target organs of skin, bone marrow and conjunctiva correlated with clinically observed toxicities and were reversible following cessation of treatment. No toxicities were observed with the unconjugated antibody (tisotumab), suggesting no impact from antigen binding alone.
L308: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L309: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Increased prothrombin time (10 to 20%) and ocular observations of glassy appearance of the fundus were observed with unconjugated tisotumab at a dose of 25 mg/kg (approximately 30-fold the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). Toxicities observed in the bone marrow, lymphoid organs (thymus, spleen), liver, gastrointestinal and male reproductive organs (tested/epididymides) are similarly seen in other approved ADCs with MMAE as the toxic payload. Skin and ocular toxicities are likely due to TF expression in the epithelial cells.
L310: ## 5.5.2 Genetic Toxicology
L311: ## **Data (presented by the FDA):**
L312: The genetic toxicology studies were initially reviewed by the FDA under the Applicant's BLA 125388 (brentuximab vedotin) and summarized below.
L313: ## **In Vitro Reverse Mutation Assay in Bacterial Cells (Ames)**
L314: **Study title/number:** Bacterial Reverse Mutation Assay: SGD-1010/AA66EH.503 (b) (4)
L315: **Key Study Findings:**
L316: - MMAE, the small molecule cytotoxic agent of tisotumab vedotin, was negative in the bacterial reverse mutation assay under the conditions tested.
L317: **GLP compliance:** Yes; except the stability of the drug and homogeneity, concentration, and stability of the drug preparations were not determined.
L318: **Test system:** MMAE was evaluated at 75, 200, 600, 1800 and 5000 µg per plate with *Salmonella typhimurium* TA98, TA100, TA1535, TA1537, and *Escherichia coli* WP2uvrA tester strains in the absence and presence of S9.
L319: **Study is valid:** Yes
L320: ## **In Vitro Assays in Mammalian Cells**
L321: **Study title/ number:** L5178Y TK+/- Mouse Lymphoma Forward Mutation Assay with a Confirmatory Assay/8204155
L322: **Key Study Findings:**
L323: - MMAE, the small molecule cytotoxic agent of tisotumab vedotin, was negative in the mouse lymphoma forward mutation assay under the conditions tested.
L324: **GLP compliance:** Yes; except, the stability and concentration of the dosing preparations were not analyzed.
L325: **Test system:** Mouse lymphoma L5178Y TK+/- cell line was treated with MMAE, ≤50 ng/mL in the absence of S9 and ≤100 ng/mL in the presence of S9, for 4-hours.
L326: **Study is valid:** Yes.
L327: ## **In Vivo Clastogenicity Assay in Rodent (Micronucleus Assay)**
L328: **Study title/number:** In Vivo Rat Bone Marrow Micronucleus Assay/8204151
L329: **Key Study Findings:**
L330: - MMAE, the small molecule cytotoxic agent of tisotumab vedotin, was positive for genotoxicity under the conditions of the assay with an aneugenic mechanism at \ge 0.1 mg/kg.
- MMAE was cytotoxic to the bone marrow at 0.2 mg/kg.
L331: GLP compliance: Yes
L332: Test system: Sprague Dawley rats were administered a single intravenous injection of MMAE at 0.01, 0.1, and 0.2 mg/kg; bone marrow micronuclei were assessed.
L333: Study is valid: Yes.
L334: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L335: MMAE was positive for genotoxicity in the in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus study through an aneugenic mechanism. MMAE was not mutagenic in the bacterial reverse mutation (Ames) assay or the L5178 TK+/ mouse lymphoma forward mutation assay.
L336: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L337: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L338: ## 5.5.3 Carcinogenicity
L339: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L340: Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted for tisotumab vedotin per ICH S6(R1), ICH S1A, and ICH S9 guidance.
L341: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L342: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position; carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support marketing for therapeutics intended to treat patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9.
L343: ## 5.5.4 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L344: ## **Data (presented by the FDA):**
L345: The Applicant assessed effects on reproductive organs as part of the GLP 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study in the monkey. Dedicated embryo-fetal developmental toxicity studies were not conducted with tisotumab vedotin. However, MMAE (SGD-1010) was evaluated in rats as part of a GLP embryo-fetal developmental and TK study that was initially reviewed by the FDA under BLA 125388 for brentuximab vedotin. Fertility and early embryonic development and pre- and post-natal studies were not needed to support the proposed advanced cancer population in accordance with ICH S9.
L346: ## **Embryo-Fetal Development**
L347: **Study title/ number:** Intravenous Injection Study for Effects on Embryofetal Developmental and Toxicokinetics with SGN-35 and SGD-1010 in Rats/8204397
L348: Key findings in rats given 0.2 mg/kg MMAE:
L349: - No viable fetuses due to post-implantation loss in 1/24 dams.
L350: - Presence of fetal external and skeletal variations and malformations.
- MMAE transferred across the placenta.
L351: GLP compliance: Yes; the characterization of SGD-1010 was performed by the Applicant under non-GLP conditions.
L352: ## Methods

Formulation/Vehicle:	SGD-1010 (MMAE) was diluted in 3.9% of 0.01N HCl and 96.1% of 0.9% sodium chloride for injection
Species/Strain:	Rat/Sprague-Dawley
Number/Sex/Group:	25 females
Satellite groups:	9 females (toxicokinetics)
Study design:	Cesarean section performed on GD 21: gross observations, uterine contents and weights, live/dead fetuses, early/late resorptions, abnormalities, number of corpora lutea. Fetal examinations included: sex, body weights, external abnormalities, visceral and skeletal examinations.
L354: Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results: No
L355: ## Observations and Results
L356: Parameters	Major findings

L358: The C_{max} and AUC_{last} for MMAE in pregnant females was 50.2 ng/mL and 25.6 ng*day/mL, respectively, on gestational day 13, which was approximately 60% higher than the values seen on gestational day 6. The mean clinical C_{max} and AUC_{0-21} for MMAE at the recommended dose
L359: was 5.9 ng/mL and 50 ng*day/mL, respectively; therefore, embryo-fetal toxicity (EFT) was seen at approximately 0.5-fold the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC. MMAE concentration in amniotic fluid and fetal serum on gestational day 18 was higher than those seen in maternal serum suggesting placental transfer. The maternal serum was below the level of quantitation for all animals; therefore, a quantitative assessment was not available.
L360: Note: Tissue factor is expressed both on the plasma membrane and intracellularly during embryonic development. TF was reported to be expressed in the cardiovascular and central nervous systems, esophagus, respiratory system, stomach, duodenum, pancreas, and liver (Luther et al. 1996). Therefore, tisotumab vedotin is likely to cause EFT from both the TF-directed antibody and the MMAE payload.
L361: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L362: ## **Fertility and Early Embryonic development**
L363: Stand-alone fertility and early embryonic studies were not performed with tisotumab vedotin. However, testicular toxicity was observed in the 13-week repeat dose study in monkeys which manifested as reduced or absent sperm motility at exposures approximately 0.6- to 4-fold times the human systemic ADC exposure (AUC) at the clinically recommended dose. There was partial recovery of the testes and epididymis findings at 3 and 5 mg/kg; and full recovery at 1 mg/kg after a 6-week postdose treatment-free period. The testicular toxicity is consistent with the pharmacologic disruption of microtubules caused by MMAE. No adverse effects were evident in the female reproductive system and menstrual cyclicity was not affected by treatment.
L364: ## **Embryofetal Development**
L365: Intravenous administration of MMAE to pregnant rats during organogenesis (gestational Days 6 and 13) resulted in malformations and embryo fetal toxicity at a dose of 0.2 mg/kg (approximately 0.5 times the human AUC at the clinically recommended dose). MMAE was transferred across the placenta and present in fetal serum. The embryo-fetal developmental toxicity is consistent with the pharmacologic disruption of microtubules caused by MMAE.
L366: ## **Pre- and Postnatal Development**
L367: In accordance with ICH S9, pre- and postnatal development studies were not conducted for tisotumab vedotin as it is intended for treatment of metastatic cervical cancer.
L368: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L369: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L370: ## 5.5.5 Other Toxicology Studies
L371: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L372: ## **Phototoxicity:**
L373: MMAE, valine-citrulline MMAE (vcMMAE), and N-acetylcysteine (NAC)-vcMMAE had no absorption in the range of 290 to 700 nm and do not present a direct phototoxicity concern.
L374: ## **Species Cross-Reactivity:**
L375: Cynomolgus monkeys were considered the relevant nonclinical test species for tisotumab vedotin based on comparable antigen binding affinities to human TF. Tisotumab vedotin did not bind to pig, dog, rabbit or rodent TF.
L376: ## **Tissue Cross-Reactivity:**
L377: Positive immunohistochemical staining was observed both on the membrane and in the cytosol of normal human and cynomolgus monkey tissues and was generally consistent with reported sites of TF expression. Staining patterns were generally similar between the tissue panels examined, although overall, staining of fewer tissue elements was present in cynomolgus monkey tissue compared to human.
L378: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L379: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L380: Data (presented by the FDA):
L381: ## **Study# (b) (4) 20023264: A Tissue Cross-Reactivity Study of IgG1-1015-011-FITC and IgG1-1015-011-vcMMAE-FITC in Normal Human and Cynomolgus Monkey Tissues (GLP compliant)**
L382: Human and cynomolgus monkey TF staining pattern (membrane) using tisotumab was generally similar across a variety of normal tissues.

L392: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L393: ## 6.1 Executive Summary
L394: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L395: Tisotumab vedotin is an antibody drug conjugate (ADC) consisting of a human tissue factor (TF)-specific monoclonal antibody linked to monomethyl auristatin E (MMAE), an anti-microtubule small molecule that induces cell cycle arrest and apoptosis, via a protease-cleavable valine-citrulline (vc) linker. The applicant is seeking accelerated approval of tisotumab vedotin(b) (4)
L396: The proposed dosing regimen of tisotumab vedotin is 2 mg/kg (up to a maximum of 200 mg in patients ≥ 100 kg) as an IV infusion over 30 minutes every 3 weeks (Q3W) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L397: The clinical pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosing regimen for the general patient population and dosing recommendations for patients with hepatic impairment (HI).
L398: Dosages ranging from 0.3-2.2 mg/kg Q3W were investigated in the dose escalation study GEN701; 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV was selected as the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) and recommended phase 2 dose (RP2D). The 2 mg/kg Q3W with a dose cap of 200 mg in patients \ge 100 kg dosing regimen was evaluated in the phase 2, single-arm, open label study GCT1015-04 (Study 04). The observed ORR was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9- 33.3%) with a DoR of 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 months, NR). Exposure-safety analyses identified a relationship between tisotumab vedotin exposure at the 2 mg/kg dose level and safety findings including the probability of treatment-related (TR) TEAEs leading to dose modifications, TR Grade \ge3 AEs, TR SAEs and grade \ge 2 ocular TEAEs. Exposure-efficacy analysis at the 2 mg/kg dose level indicated that over the range of exposures achieved at this dose level, lower exposure was correlated with lower ORR. Despite limited evaluation of the benefit-risk profile of dosages lower than 2 mg/kg, further evaluation of alternative dosing regimens to mitigate the high incidence of ocular toxicities at the proposed dose was not recommended, as lower exposures may lead to lower efficacy, based on exposure-response relationships at the 2 mg/kg dose level. As further lowering of efficacy is undesirable given the currently observed ORR and DoR, and considering the late-line setting, the proposed dose was assessed as acceptable for the proposed recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer indication.
L399: Population PK analysis indicated that patients with mild HI had higher MMAE exposure (37% higher average concentration over a dose interval i.e. Cave) compared to patients with normal function. However, no dose modification is necessary because this increase in MMAE exposure was not associated with a substantial decrease in tolerability of tisotumab vedotin in patients with mild HI. As unconjugated MMAE exposures are expected to increase in moderate and severe hepatic impairment and the proposed dosage of tisotumab vedotin was identified as the MTD, patients with moderate or severe HI should avoid treatment with tisotumab vedotin.
L400: ## Recommendations
L401: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology recommends the approval of BLA 761208 from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below:
L402: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	Primary evidence of the effectiveness of tisotumab vedotin in the proposed indication is derived from Study 04. The observed ORR by IRC (primary endpoint) was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9- 33.3%). BORs included 7 patients (6.9%) with CR and 17 patients (16.8%) with
L403: PR. The median DOR was 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 months, NR) by IRC.
General dosing instructions	The proposed recommended dosing regimen of tisotumab vedotin is 2 mg/kg Q3W (up to 200 mg in patients \ge 100 kg), as a 30 min IV infusion.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dose modification is recommended for patients with mild HI (NCI-ODWG criteria). Tisotumab vedotin should be avoided in moderate and severe HI.
No dose modification is recommended for patients with mild to moderate renal impairment (CLcr \ge 30 mL/min). There are no data in patients with severe renal impairment (CLcr < 30 mL/min).
No tisotumab vedotin dose modification is recommended when used concomitantly with CYP3A inhibitors.

Labeling	The clinical pharmacology review team has specific content and formatting change recommendations for the product labeling. Key modifications to the label made by the FDA include the recommendation to avoid dosing in moderate and severe HI in Section 8.6, and the format and content in Section 12.3.
Bridge between the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations	During development, there were no changes to the formulation.
L404: ## 6.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L405: ## 6.2.1 Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L406: ## **Data:**
L407: Following tisotumab vedotin IV infusion, peak tisotumab vedotin concentrations were attained at the end of infusion. Plasma concentrations of free MMAE increased until ~2-3 days after tisotumab vedotin dosing. No accumulation of tisotumab vedotin or free MMAE was observed on repeat dosing.
L408: Based on population PK, the typical value of nonspecific linear clearance (CL) of tisotumab vedotin was 1.42 L/day. Following a tisotumab vedotin dose of 2.0 mg/kg, ~ 60% of tisotumab vedotin was eliminated by linear CL. Terminal median half-lives were 4.04 days for tisotumab vedotin and 2.56 days for MMAE, respectively. Following 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin IV, the typical values for tisotumab vedotin central volume of distribution and peripheral volume of distribution were 3.10 L and 4.47 L, respectively.
L409: A small fraction of MMAE released from tisotumab vedotin is metabolized. In vitro data indicates that metabolism of MMAE occurs primarily by CYP3A4. In vitro, the binding of MMAE to human plasma proteins ranged from 67.9% to 82.2%. MMAE is not likely to displace or to be displaced by highly protein-bound drugs. After a single dose of brentuximab vedotin, another ADC that contains MMAE, ~24% of the total MMAE administered was recovered in feces and urine over a 1-week period (Han 2013), ~72% of which was recovered unchanged in the feces. A similar excretion profile for MMAE is expected after tisotumab vedotin administration.
L410: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L411: Population PK indicated that distribution of tisotumab vedotin was limited to the central vascular compartment, with a median elimination half-life of ~4.04 days and no accumulation upon repeat dosing. MMAE has a mean elimination half-life of ~2.56 days with no accumulation on repeat dosing. MMAE exhibited moderate binding to plasma proteins. MMAE is metabolized predominantly by CYP3A4 and excreted mainly by hepatobiliary routes.
L412: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L413: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L414: ## 6.2.2 General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L415: ## 6.2.2.1 General Dosing
L416: ## **Data:**
L417: The dose escalation part in GEN701 evaluated tisotumab vedotin at doses ranging from 0.3 to 2.6 mg/kg. At the 2.2 mg/kg dose level, 3 subjects experienced 1 dose-limiting toxicity (DLT) each. Because the protocol specified that intermediate dose levels could be evaluated, the DMC recommended to include 3 subjects on 2.0 mg/kg, which was not a preplanned dose level in the protocol. None of the 3 subjects on the 2.0 mg/kg dose level experienced DLTs and, therefore, 2.0 mg/kg was identified as the recommended phase 2 dose (RP2D) for the expansion part.
L418: In the pivotal trial GCT1015-04, in subjects treated with 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, the probability of response significantly increased with increase of tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 C_{max}. The exposure-efficacy relationships explored in the analyses indicate an increase of efficacy with increase in tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 exposure, however, responses were observed across the entire exposure range. Lower risk of death is associated with increase of tisotumab vedotin exposure. DOR and PFS were not correlated with ADC exposure (full covariate model).
L419: Weight-based dosing at 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W up to 200 mg for body weight \ge100 kg ensured exposure was similar across tertiles of weight for both ADC and MMAE and is therefore an appropriate dosing paradigm for tisotumab vedotin.
L420: Tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposure and safety endpoint relationships suggest increased exposure was associated with increased incidence of AEs, but that 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge100 kg) was tolerated with a high relative dose intensity of 96% and overall manageable safety profile.
L421: Overall, tisotumab vedotin administered at 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge 100 kg), demonstrates robust activity in patients with cervical cancer. This dosing regimen was selected based on the benefit/risk ratio observed in the Phase 1 clinical trial GEN701 where a range of doses was tested. While activity was observed at lower dose levels, 2.0 mg/kg was associated with greater antitumor activity compared to lower doses, with a manageable safety profile.
L422: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L423: The recommended clinical dose of tisotumab vedotin is 2.0 mg/kg administered by IV infusion 1Q3W. Tisotumab vedotin at 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W had a manageable safety profile and provided clinical benefit to subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer.
L424: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L425: FDA agrees that the proposed 2 mg/kg Q3W IV with a cap of 200 mg for patients \ge 100 kg is acceptable for the general population. However, FDA notes that benefit-risk balance at doses lower than the proposed 2 mg/kg Q3W was not adequately characterized due to limited sample sizes in the lower dose cohorts. In Study GEN701, n=3 patients were evaluated at all dose levels (0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.2, 1.5, 1.8 and 2 mg/kg) other than 2.2 mg/kg where n=6 patients were evaluated. Of note, in the dose escalation phase of Study GEN 701, only one patient responded at the 1.2 mg/kg dose level and there were no responders at the proposed 2 mg/kg dose. Despite limited evaluation of the benefit-risk profile of dosages lower than 2 mg/kg, further evaluation of alternative dosing regimens to mitigate the high incidence of ocular toxicities at the proposed dose was not recommended, as lower exposures may lead to lower efficacy based on limited exposure-response relationship assessment at only the 2 mg/kg Q3W dosage. Further lowering of efficacy is undesirable given the currently observed ORR and DoR, and the late-line setting.
L426: ## 6.2.2.2 Therapeutic Individualization
L427: ## **Data:**
L428: Tisotumab vedotin has been administered based on subject body weight, with the exception of subjects \ge 100 kg for which a dose cap (200 mg) is in place.
L429: Impact of intrinsic factors on tisotumab vedotin and MMAE PK was assessed in the population PK model. The final model included body weight, baseline serum albumin, and sex as covariates at tisotumab vedotin PK and age, sex, body weight, cancer type, albumin, estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) status, hepatic impairment and baseline tumor burden as covariates for MMAE PK. The model-predicted cycle 1 average serum concentration (C_{ave}) values were evaluated for these covariates following 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge 100 kg). Comparison of exposures indicated no meaningful difference in tisotumab vedotin or MMAE exposures between male and female subjects, or across the age range. Subjects with higher weight had slightly higher tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures, with C_{ave} values for ADC (C_{ave,ADC}) being 5% higher in
L430: the highest tertile of weight and C_{ave} values for MMAE (C_{ave,MMAE}) being 18% higher. The range of effects relative to a reference population was within the observed variability in the modeling dataset for both tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE.
L431: A population PK analysis of the effects of hepatic function on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE was conducted in subjects with mild hepatic impairment at baseline, as determined by the criteria of the NCI Organ Dysfunction Working Group. Predicted tisotumab vedotin exposure for patients with mild hepatic impairment was similar to patients with normal hepatic function, while MMAE exposures were 37% higher. The range of both tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures was similar between the 2 groups. Subjects who had mild hepatic impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent serious adverse events (SAEs), or grade \ge 3 treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), indicating the increase of MMAE exposure in mild hepatic impairment patients is not clinically meaningful.
L432: A population PK analysis of the effects of renal function on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE was conducted in subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment based on estimated creatinine clearance (CrCl) at baseline as determined using the Cockcroft-Gault Formula (Cockcroft 1976). Predicted tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment were similar to patients with normal renal function. Subjects who had mild or moderate renal impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent SAEs, or grade \ge 3 TEAEs.
L433: Formal drug-drug interaction (DDI) studies with tisotumab vedotin have not been conducted. In vitro data and clinical drug-drug interaction study data from brentuximab vedotin (Han 2013), an ADC structurally similar to tisotumab vedotin, were used to support the clinical pharmacology evaluations of tisotumab vedotin. In the clinical DDI study with brentuximab vedotin, concomitant use of ketoconazole led to a 34% increase in free MMAE AUC exposure, while concomitant use of rifampicin was associated with a 46% decrease in free MMAE AUC exposure. The pharmacokinetics of brentuximab vedotin were unchanged by either drug (Han 2013). Concomitant use of strong inhibitors or inducers of CYP3A4 with tisotumab vedotin may result in changes in free MMAE exposure, however the magnitude observed for brentuximab vedotin was not considered clinically meaningful.
L434: ## The Applicant's Position:
L435: Dose individualization is recommended on the basis of body weight, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg for subjects treated at 2.0 mg/kg with body weight of 100 kg or greater. Population PK modeling demonstrated body weight-based dosing is appropriate as resulting exposures were generally similar across tertiles of weight for both tisotumab vedotin and MMAE.
L436: Dose adjustment is not required based on age or sex. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild hepatic impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been evaluated in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been studied in patients with severe renal impairment. No dose adjustment is required with concomitant administration of
L437: tisotumab vedotin with strong inhibitors/inducers of CYP3A4. Closely monitor for adverse reactions when tisotumab vedotin is given concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors. The safety and effectiveness of tisotumab vedotin in pediatric patients has not been established.
L438: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L439: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA recommends that tisotumab vedotin be avoided in patients with moderate and severe HI. This is because unconjugated MMAE exposures are expected to increase in moderate and severe hepatic impairment and the proposed dosage of tisotumab vedotin was identified as the MTD. Evaluation of lower doses in patients with moderate and severe HI was not recommended, as lowering the dose of tisotumab vedotin to match the exposure of MMAE may lead to lower ADC concentrations which could lead to lower efficacy (based on known exposure-response relationships of the ADC with efficacy).
L440: ## 6.2.2.3 Outstanding Issues
L441: ## **Data:**
L442: N/A
L443: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L444: N/A

L446: There are no outstanding issues.
L447: ## 6.3 Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L448: ## 6.3.1 General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L449: ## **Data:**
L450: The clinical pharmacology of tisotumab vedotin has been studied in subjects with solid tumors who received tisotumab vedotin by IV infusion 1Q3W. Levels of ADC, TAB, and free MMAE were measured using validated methods to evaluate PK parameters. The general pharmacology and PK characteristics are summarized in Table 3.
L451: **Table 3:** General pharmacology and PK characteristics of tisotumab vedotin
L452: 2a. General Information
Proposed indication	Recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer that progressed on or after chemotherapy
Route and formulation type and formulation strengths	IV infusion
Formulation Strengths: 40 mg/vial
Planned dose	2.0 mg/kg (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge 100 kg) 1Q3W
Mechanism of action	MMAE-mediated disruption of microtubules and bystander cytotoxicity in neighboring tumor cells
2b. Dose and Adverse Events
L453: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L454: Therapeutic dose and exposure	The proposed clinical dosing regimen of tisotumab vedotin is 2.0 mg/kg (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge 100 kg) administered on day 1 of each 3-week (21 day) treatment cycle.
Based on population PK analysis the predicted mean (STD) first cycle exposures are:
ADC: C_{max}: 40.8 (8.12) \mug/mL; AUC_{0-21}: 57.5 (13.4) day*\mug/mL
MMAE: C_{max}: 5.91 (4.2) ng/mL; AUC_{0-21}: 50 (35.8) day*ng/mL
Maximum tolerated dose	2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W
Principal adverse events	The most common TEAEs in subjects with cervical cancer were epistaxis, nausea, fatigue, and alopecia.
2c. PK Features
Maximum dose tested	Single Dose	2.2 mg/kg 1Q3W
Multiple Dose	2.2 mg/kg 1Q3W until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity
Exposures Achieved at Maximum Tested Dose	First Cycle	ADC:
Geometric Mean (%CV) at 2.2 mg/kg 1Q3W (n=6)
C_{max}: 55.5 (10.31%) \mug/mL; AUC_{0-t}: 84.9 (33.7%) day*\mug/mL
MMAE:
Geometric Mean (%CV) at 2.2 mg/kg 1Q3W (n=6)
C_{max}: 4.88 (31.4%) ng/mL; AUC_{0-t}: 26.5 (59.4%)
Range of linear PK	Following the first dose of tisotumab vedotin, tisotumab vedotin C_{max} increased in an approximately dose proportional manner, while AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-\infty} increased in a more than dose-proportional manner, across a 0.3–2.2 mg/kg dose range.
Accumulation at steady-state	Based on Population PK analysis there was no accumulation of ADC or MMAE following 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W dosing.
Metabolites	Tisotumab vedotin is an ADC composed of a human tissue factor specific human monoclonal IgG1 antibody, conjugated to the microtubule-disrupting agent MMAE via a protease-cleavable vc maleimidocaproyl linker.Clearance of the IgG1 antibody is believed to be similar to endogenous IgG1, ie, catabolism to small peptides and individual amino acids.
The metabolism of MMAE has been studied in vitro following incubation with human liver microsomes. Five key metabolic pathways were identified: N-demethylation (CYP3A4), O-demethylation (CYP3A4), dehydrogenation (CYP3A4), amide hydrolysis, and oxidation. Qualitative analysis confirmed the presence of several MMAE metabolites in clinical samples from patients who received brentuximab vedotin.

Absorption	Absolute/Relative Bioavailability	Not applicable.
T_{max}	Median (range) at 2.0 mg/kg based on population PK analysis:
MMAE: 2.62 days (0.146–3.71)
Distribution	V_d/F or V_d	Based on population PK analysis, the typical value of central volume of distribution of tisotumab vedotin was estimated to be 3.10 L, and the typical value of peripheral volume of distribution was estimated to be 4.47 L.
% bound	In vitro, the binding of MMAE to human plasma proteins ranged from 68% to 82%.
L455: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L456: Elimination	Route	Tisotumab vedotin and its unconjugated antibody are eliminated from the systemic circulation by cellular uptake processes, while the elimination of unconjugated MMAE occurs primarily by hepatic and to a lesser extent by renal mechanisms of clearance. Nonclinical studies in rat showed unchanged MMAE was excreted primarily in feces after MMAE administration.
t_{1/2}	Median (range) at 2.0 mg/kg based on population PK analysis:
ADC: 4.04 (2.26 - 7.25) days
CL/F or CL	Based on population PK analysis, the typical value of nonspecific linear clearance of tisotumab vedotin in subjects was estimated to be 1.42 L/day. Following a 2.0 mg/kg dose, about 60% of the ADC was estimated to be eliminated by linear CL.
Intrinsic Factors	Age	Not clinically significant
Sex	Not clinically significant
Race	Could not be assessed
Hepatic and Renal Impairment	Based on population PK analysis, no differences in ADC or MMAE exposures have been observed between patients with mild or moderate renal impairment and patients with normal renal function.
The effect of severe renal impairment on the PK of tisotumab vedotin is unknown.
Based on population PK analysis, no differences in ADC exposures were observed between patients with mild hepatic impairment and patients with normal hepatic function. Free MMAE exposures were 37% higher in subjects with mild hepatic impairment compared to subjects with normal hepatic function.
The effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the PK of tisotumab vedotin is unknown.

Drug interactions	Drug interaction assessments have not been conducted with tisotumab vedotin. In clinical drug-drug interaction studies with brentuximab vedotin, concomitant administration of ketoconazole with brentuximab vedotin led to a 34% increase in free MMAE AUC exposure, while concomitant administration of rifampicin was associated with a 46% decrease in free MMAE AUC exposure. The pharmacokinetics of midazolam were not affected by concomitant administration with brentuximab vedotin.
Meal Effects	Not applicable
Population PK Analyses	The final population PK model included body weight, baseline serum albumin, and sex as covariates for tisotumab vedotin PK and age, sex, body weight, cancer type, albumin, eGFR, ECOG, hepatic impairment and baseline tumor burden as covariates for MMAE PK.
2d. Pharmacodynamic Features
Tisotumab vedotin at 2.0 mg/kg (1Q3W) did not appear to have adverse effects on ventricular repolarization and did not result in a clinically meaningful prolongation of the QTc interval.
As with all therapeutic proteins, there is potential for an immune response to tisotumab vedotin. In GCT1015-04, a total of 93 patients were tested for immunogenicity to tisotumab vedotin; 5 patients (5%) developed treatment-emergent anti-tisotumab vedotin antibodies. Neutralizing anti-tisotumab vedotin antibodies were detected in 2 patients. Across clinical trials, 4.3% (13/305) of patients developed treatment-emergent anti-tisotumab vedotin antibodies. The presence of anti-tisotumab vedotin antibodies had no clinically meaningful effect on the pharmacokinetics of tisotumab vedotin.
L457: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L458: Based on population PK analysis, the terminal half-lives of tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE were 4.04 days and 2.56 days, respectively. The incidence of antidrug antibodies (ADA) in GCT1015-04 was low with no apparent effect on tisotumab vedotin PK.
L459: No dedicated clinical drug-drug interaction studies with tisotumab vedotin in humans were conducted. However, a clinical drug-drug interaction study was conducted with another ADC that contains MMAE, and similar effects would be expected for tisotumab vedotin. Based on clinical data of brentuximab vedotin, concomitant use of strong inhibitors of CYP3A4 with tisotumab vedotin may result in an increase in free MMAE exposure. Monitoring for adverse reactions is recommended when tisotumab vedotin is given concomitantly with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L460: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L461: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment provided in the data section above. Additional relevant information that is not included in the Applicant's assessment or where the FDA's assessment differs from the Applicant's, is summarized below in Table 4.
L462: **Table 4 General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics**
L463: Chemical structure and molecular weight	Tisotumab vedotin is an ADC consisting of a TF-specific human IgG1-κ monoclonal antibody bound to the microtubule-disrupting agent cytotoxic drug MMAE, via a protease-cleavable valine-citrulline (vc) linker. The average drug-to-antibody ratio is approximately 4. The molecular weight of tisotumab vedotin is approximately 153 kDa.
QT/QTc prolongation	Based on the FDA QT IRT's analysis, tisotumab vedotin treatment at 2 mg/kg Q3W IV does not cause large mean increases (>20 msec) in the QTc interval (Source: FDA QT IRT review).
Bioanalytical assay	ADC (tisotumab vedotin), total antibody and unconjugated MMAE concentrations were measured in Studies GEN701, GEN702, 04 and SGNTV-001. ELISA assays were used to measure tisotumab vedotin concentrations, which utilized TFECDHs as a capture reagent and an anti-MMAE antibody for detection. ELISA assays were used to measure total antibody concentrations, which utilized TFECDHs as a capture reagent and an anti-hIgG1 antibody for detection. Plasma unconjugated MMAE concentrations were measured using liquid chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS). Please refer to section 19.4.1 for further information.
Dose proportionality	Tisotumab vedotin undergoes both linear and non-linear elimination. Following the first dose of tisotumab vedotin, ADC Cmax increased in a dose-proportional manner while AUC0-t increased in a more than dose-proportional manner over the 0.3–2.2 mg/kg dose range.

Immunogenicity	
Please see OBP review regarding the bioanalytical performance of the ADA and neutralizing antibody assays.
In Study 04, 93 patients with cervical cancer treated at the proposed dosage had immunogenicity assessment at baseline and at least one time point post-dose. Five of 93 (5.4%) patients developed treatment-emergent ADA against tisotumab vedotin. Of these 5 patients, 2 patients first tested positive for treatment-emergent ADA at C4, 1 patient first tested positive at C7 and 2 patients first tested positive at the treatment discontinuation visit. Two of these 5 patients in Study 04, tested positive for neutralizing antibodies against tisotumab vedotin.
L465: Across studies, 145 patients with cervical cancer treated at the proposed dosage in Studies GEN701, GEN702, 04 had immunogenicity assessment at baseline and at least one time point post-dose. Eight of 145 patients (5.5%) developed treatment-emergent ADA against tisotumab vedotin.
Due the limited number of patients who were positive for ADA during treatment with tisotumab vedotin, the impact of ADA on the PK, safety and efficacy of tisotumab vedotin is not fully characterized.

Drug interaction liability	
In vitro:
MMAE is a substrate of CYP3A4 and P-gp
MMAE does not inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, or CYP2D6, but is an inhibitor of CYP3A
MMAE does not induce CYP450 enzymes
MMAE does not inhibit P-gp at clinically relevant concentrations
No clinical drug-drug interaction studies have been conducted with tisotumab vedotin. Findings in clinical DDI studies with another MMAE-containing ADC, brentuximab vedotin, are expected to be relevant to tisotumab vedotin. In clinical drug-drug interaction studies, concomitant administration of a strong CYP3A inhibitor ketoconazole with brentuximab vedotin increased unconjugated MMAE Cmax by 25% and AUC by 34%, with no change in ADC exposure. Concomitant administration of a strong CYP3A4 inducer rifampicin with brentuximab vedotin decreased unconjugated MMAE Cmax by 44% and AUC by 46%, with no change in ADC exposure. The pharmacokinetics of a sensitive CYP3A substrate midazolam were not affected by concomitant administration with brentuximab vedotin.
L466: ## 6.3.2 Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L467: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L468: Yes, the clinical pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of tisotumab vedotin effectiveness in patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer.
L469: ## **Data:**
L470: To support the clinical efficacy observed with tisotumab vedotin dosed at 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, exposure-efficacy response was evaluated in Study GCT1015-04 for the primary endpoint of ORR and secondary endpoints of PFS, OS, and DOR (m2.7.2, Section 3.6.4.2).
L471: Logistic regression analysis determined a positive relationship between tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 C_{max} and ORR. Tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 AUC was also a predictor of ORR in the base model but not significant in the full covariate model. There was a relationship of increased probability of response with an increase in tisotumab vedotin C_{max}, but responses were observed across the exposure tertiles. In the lowest exposure tertile, an ORR of 14.7% was observed, indicating that even patients within the lowest tertile of exposure have the potential to derive benefit from tisotumab vedotin (Report No. SR1015-249). DOR and PFS were not correlated with ADC exposure in the full covariate model. The relationships between tisotumab vedotin exposure and efficacy endpoints suggest that treatment with 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin was associated with clinically meaningful efficacy across the entire range of tisotumab vedotin concentrations in recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer (m2.7.2, Section 3.6.4.4).
L472: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L473: The relationships between tisotumab vedotin exposure and efficacy endpoints show that treatment with 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W was associated with clinically meaningful efficacy across the entire range of concentrations in subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who have progressed on or after chemotherapy.
L474: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L475: The FDA agrees that the clinical pharmacology program provides evidence of efficacy at the 2 mg/kg Q3W IV dosage in the proposed indication.
L476: The Applicant's exposure-response analyses support the proposed dose. Exposure-safety analysis included the data from 272 subjects administered tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV in studies GEN701 (expansion cohort), GEN702 (expansion cohort), and GCT1015-04. Exposure-efficacy analysis included the data from 101 subjects from Study GCT1015-04. As the population PK analysis showed that there was no appreciable accumulation of ADC and MMAE with repeated dosing, Cycle 1 exposure measures were used for all exposure-response analyses. The exposure-response analyses indicate relationships with both safety and efficacy at the range of exposures achieved after the first cycle of treatment at the 2 mg/kg Q3W IV dosage (please refer to the pharmacometrics review in section 19.4.2 for a detailed analysis).
L477: Exposure-efficacy analyses indicated that the probability of achieving an objective response increased with increasing ADC exposure Figure 1.
L478: **Figure 1: ADC exposure-response analysis for ORR at 2mg/kg**
L479: Source: Applicant's exposure-response analysis
L480: Exposure-safety analyses indicated that increasing tisotumab vedotin exposure at the proposed dosage led to increased probability of treatment-related (TR) TEAEs leading to dose modifications, TR Grade \ge 3 AEs, TR SAEs and grade \ge 2 ocular TEAEs Figure 2.
L481: Figure 2: ADC exposure-response analysis for Grade \ge 2 ocular toxicity at 2mg/kg
L482: Source: Applicant's exposure-response analysis
L483: Overall, while interpretation of the exposure-response relationships is limited by the fact that these analyses are limited to exposures at a single dose level, these findings suggest that lower doses might be less toxic but also less efficacious.
L484: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L485: Yes, tisotumab vedotin administered by IV infusion at 2.0 mg/kg, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg 1Q3W is appropriate for patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the observed efficacy, safety, and PK data.
L486: ## Data:
L487: During dose escalation in the Phase 1 trial GEN701, the highest dose evaluated was 2.2 mg/kg 1Q3W, but due to 3 DLTs (diabetes mellitus type II, mucositis and neutropenic fever, all grade 3) reported in 3 subjects, the dose was reduced to 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, which was defined as the maximum tolerated dose and RP2D. Tisotumab vedotin doses were administered based on subject body weight, except for subjects &gt;100 kg.
L488: Logistic regression analysis, performed as part of the efficacy-response analysis, determined a positive relationship between tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 Cmax and ORR (Report No. SR1015-249). Although there was a relationship of increased probability of response with an increase in tisotumab vedotin Cmax, responses were observed across the exposure tertiles. In the lowest
L489: exposure tertile, an ORR of 14.7% was observed, indicating that even patients within the lowest tertile of exposure have the potential to derive benefit from tisotumab vedotin. The risk of death significantly decreased with increase of tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 AUC and Cycle 1 C_{max} exposure. However, DOR and PFS were not correlated with tisotumab vedotin exposure in the full covariate model.
L490: Tisotumab vedotin exposure and safety relationships were evaluated in a safety population comprising subjects treated at the 2.0 mg/kg dose. Logistic regression analyses suggested that tisotumab vedotin exposure (cycle 1 AUC and C_{max}) was a predictor of the probability of treatment-related TEAEs leading to dose interruptions; tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 AUC was a predictor of treatment-related TEAEs leading to dose reductions; tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 AUC, Cycle 1 C_{max} and C_{aveLast} were predictors of treatment-related TEAEs leading to dose discontinuations. Tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 C_{max} was a predictor of the probability of treatment-related grade 3 or higher AEs. Tisotumab vedotin Cycle 1 AUC, Cycle 1 C_{max} and C_{aveLast} were predictors of the probability of grade 2 and higher ocular AEs. In a sub-analysis of the GCT1015-04 trial, where a comprehensive OMP was used, tisotumab vedotin exposure (Cycle 1 AUC, Cycle 1 C_{max}, and C_{aveLast}) were predictors of the probability of grade 2 and higher ocular AEs. Logistic regression analyses suggested that MMAE Cycle 1 AUC was a predictor of the probability of treatment-related SAEs and all SAEs. Logistic regression analyses suggested that neither tisotumab vedotin or MMAE exposures were predictors of the probability of grade 2 or higher bleeding AEs or grade 2 or higher peripheral neuropathy. Although logistic regression analyses revealed relationships between tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures and reported safety outcomes, treatment with 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin was associated with a manageable safety profile across the entire range of concentrations in subjects with solid tumors.
L491: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L492: Tisotumab vedotin administered by IV infusion at 2.0 mg/kg, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg 1Q3W demonstrates a favorable benefit-risk profile for patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer.
L493: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L494: In general, the FDA agrees that the proposed dosing regimen of 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg in patients \ge 100 kg, is acceptable for the general patient population in the recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer indication. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by efficacy data in the pivotal Study 04, and the exposure-response relationships of tisotumab vedotin with safety and efficacy at this dosage.
L495: Selection of the proposed tisotumab vedotin dosage was based on findings in the phase 1/2 clinical trial, Study GEN701. Dosing regimens ranging from 0.3 to 2.2 mg/kg Q3W IV (0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.2, 1.5, 1.8, 2.0 and 2.2 mg/kg; n=3 in all cohorts except 2.2 mg/kg where n=6) were evaluated in patients with advanced or metastatic solid tumors, using a 3+3 design in the phase 1 dose escalation portion of Study GEN701. As 50% of the patients at the 2.2 mg/kg dose level
L496: experienced DLTs, 3 patients were treated at the 2.0 mg/kg dose level. As no patients experienced DLTs at this dose level, the 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV dosage was identified as the MTD and the RP2D for further evaluation in the dose expansion phase 2. Of note, only 1 confirmed PR was observed in the dose escalation phase, at the 1.2 mg/kg dose level. In the dose expansion phase of Study GEN701, 168 patients with solid tumors were treated at the 2 mg/kg Q3W IV dosage, including 55 patients with cervical cancer. An ORR of 22% (95% CI: 11.5-36%) was observed in a subset of 50 cervical cancer patients in the dose expansion phase who had received 1 or more prior lines of systemic therapy for recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. The FDA notes that dose-response analyses with both safety and efficacy are limited by the small number of patients treated at dosages &lt;2 mg/kg Q3W IV. Therefore, there are limited clinical safety and efficacy data at other dose levels to conclude that the benefit-risk balance at the proposed 2 mg/kg Q3W IV dosing regimen is optimal.
L497: Primary evidence of clinical benefit in the proposed indication at the dosage of 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg in patients \ge 100 kg, is derived from in Study 04 in 101 patients with cervical cancer. In Study 04, the observed ORR by IRC (primary endpoint) was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9- 33.3 with a median DOR was 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 months, NR) by IRC. While a relatively high incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs (60.4%) and all Grade ocular toxicities (54.5%) was observed at the proposed dosage in Study 04, the mean (\pm SD) relative dose intensity remained high at 91 (\pm13) %. In addition, the median time to first dose modification due to a TEAE was 3.1 months. This is of relevance as the median time to response in Study 04 was 1.4 months (range, 1.1 to 5.1 months) and the majority of responders (19/24 patients with CR or PR), had responses early i.e. at the first post-baseline scan, within the first two months of treatment. In addition, the majority of dose discontinuations were due to progressive disease, with 13/101 (12.9%) of patients requiring dose discontinuation due to TEAEs.
L498: Additionally, as stated in section 6.3.2.1, the Applicant's exposure-response analyses support the proposed dose. Exposure-efficacy analyses indicated that the probability of achieving an objective response increased with increasing ADC exposure. Exposure-safety analyses indicated that increasing tisotumab vedotin exposure at the proposed dosage led to increased probability of treatment-related (TR) TEAEs leading to dose modifications, TR Grade \ge3 AEs, TR SAEs and grade \ge 2 ocular TEAEs.
L499: Overall, the current exposure-response analyses suggest that lower doses might be less toxic but also less efficacious. As part of the review, FDA also considered whether alternative dosing approaches such as fractionated dosing or a pre-specified dose reduction after the initial cycles might help mitigate the high incidence of ocular toxicities observed at the proposed dosage. FDA concluded that evaluating lower doses or alternative regimens may have utility to lower toxicities but may also potentially further lower the efficacy of tisotumab vedotin which, given the current ORR and DoR, was assessed as unacceptable. Overall, given the considerations above, the proposed patient population i.e. patients with metastatic or
L500: recurrent cervical cancer after 1 or 2 prior systemic treatment regimens for their recurrent or metastatic disease, and the limitations of available alternative therapeutic options in this setting, the benefit-risk balance at the proposed dose level in this indication was assessed as acceptable.
L501: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L502: No, tisotumab vedotin administered by IV infusion at 2.0 mg/kg, up to a maximum dose of 200 mg 1Q3W is appropriate for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors.
L503: ## **Data:**
L504: The effects of intrinsic factors on tisotumab vedotin and MMAE PK were assessed in the population PK model. The model identified body weight, baseline serum albumin, and sex as covariates for tisotumab vedotin PK and age, sex, body weight, cancer type, albumin, eGFR, ECOG, hepatic impairment and baseline tumor burden as covariates for MMAE PK. The model-predicted cycle 1 C_{ave} values were evaluated for these covariates following 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin (up to 200 mg for body weight \ge 100 kg). Comparison of exposures indicated no meaningful difference in ADC or MMAE exposures between male and female subjects, or across the age range. Subjects with higher weight had slightly higher tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures, with C_{ave,ADC} being 5% higher in the highest tertile of weight and C_{ave,MMAE} being 18% higher. The range of effects relative to a reference population was within the observed variability in the modeling dataset for both tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE.
L505: A population PK analysis of the effects of hepatic function on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE was conducted in subjects with mild hepatic impairment at baseline, as determined by the criteria of the NCI Organ Dysfunction Working Group. Predicted tisotumab vedotin exposure for patients with mild hepatic impairment was similar to patients with normal hepatic function, while MMAE exposures were 37% higher. The range of both tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures was similar between the 2 groups. Subjects who had mild hepatic impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent SAEs, or grade \ge 3 TEAEs, relative to those with normal hepatic function, indicating the increase of MMAE exposure in mild hepatic impairment patients is not clinically meaningful.
L506: A population PK analysis of the effects of renal function on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE was conducted in subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment based on estimated CrCl at baseline as determined using the Cockcroft-Gault Formula (Cockcroft 1976). Predicted tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment were similar to patients with normal renal function. Subjects who had mild or moderate renal impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent SAEs, or grade \ge 3 TEAEs, relative to subjects with normal renal function.
L507: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L508: Tisotumab vedotin dose adjustment is not required for subjects with mild or moderate renal impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been evaluated in subjects with severe renal
L509: impairment. Dose adjustment is also not required for subjects with mild hepatic impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been evaluated in subjects with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. Based on population PK analysis, no dose adjustment is required for the intrinsic factors of age or sex.
L510: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L511: FDA agrees there is no clinically meaningful effect of age (21 to 81 years), sex, race (White vs Non-White) or ethnicity (Hispanic vs Non-Hispanic) on tisotumab vedotin or unconjugated MMAE exposure. Population PK analysis identified body weight to be a statistically significant covariate on clearance and volume of the distribution of tisotumab vedotin, which supports the bodyweight-based dosing regimen. ADC and MMAE Cave were similar in patients with body weight &lt; 100 kg and \ge 100 kg (Figure 3), supporting the dose cap of 200 mg for patients \ge 100 kg.
L512: **Figure 3: Model-predicted ADC and MMAE exposures Cycle 1 versus tertiles of weight and in patients &lt; 100 kg vs \ge 100 kg (2 mg/kg Q3W with 200 mg Cap)**
L513: Source: Applicant's PopPK report, Figure 110
L514: Based on exploratory analyses with observed PK data in Study 04, baseline tumor H-score (n=80) did not appear to impact tisotumab vedotin concentration at the end of infusion or MMAE Ctrough after the first dose. Circulating tissue factor (cTF) was not included in the population PK model, but exploratory analyses based on the available matched PK and cTF data from subjects (N= 283) in studies GEN701, GEN702 and GCT1015-04, indicated that cTF did not impact tisotumab vedotin clearance or central volume of distribution. In addition, based on the
L515: observed concentrations of cTF, the highest cTF molar concentration was much lower (~ 222-fold) than the predicted lower range of the ADC molar Cave concentration. Given the markedly higher concentrations of ADC to relative cTF, minimal impact to clearance might be expected.
L516: ## Hepatic impairment:
L517: No dose modification is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment. No formal clinical trial has been conducted to assess the effect of hepatic function on the PK of tisotumab vedotin. The effect of mild hepatic impairment on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE exposure was assessed using the population PK model. The population PK analysis included patients with mild (n=58) hepatic impairment, in addition to patients with normal (n=341) hepatic function. Hepatic impairment status was identified as a covariate for MMAE clearance in the Applicant's population PK model. Model-predicted Cycle 1 ADC exposure in patients with mild hepatic impairment was similar to patients with normal hepatic function, while MMAE exposures were 37% higher in patients with mild hepatic impairment (Figure 4).
L518: **Figure 4: Model-predicted ADC and MMAE Cycle 1 exposures (2.0 mg/kg Q3W with 200 mg cap) in mild hepatic impairment vs normal hepatic function**
L519: Source: Applicant's PopPK Report SR1015-248, Figure 113
L520: Comparison of safety by baseline hepatic function (per the NCI-ODWG criteria) across all subjects who received tisotumab vedotin at 2.0 mg/kg Q3W IV in studies GCT1015-04, GEN701, SGNTV-001, GCT1015-03, and GEN702 (Table 5) indicated no marked decrease in overall tolerability in mild hepatic impairment. However, patients with mild hepatic impairment had a higher incidence of liver enzyme (total bilirubin, AST and ALT) increases.

L524: Given that there is no substantial decrease in the tolerability of tisotumab vedotin in patients with mild hepatic impairment, and as lowering the dose of tisotumab vedotin to match the exposure of MMAE may lead to lower ADC concentrations which could lead to lower efficacy (based on the exposure-response analysis with efficacy), no tisotumab vedotin dose adjustment is recommended for this population.
L525: The effect of moderate or severe hepatic impairment on the PK and safety of tisotumab vedotin has not been assessed. However, unconjugated MMAE exposures are expected to further increase in the setting of moderate and severe hepatic impairment. As the proposed dosage of tisotumab vedotin was identified as the MTD and unconjugated MMAE exposure is correlated with increased SAEs based on exposure-safety analyses, tisotumab vedotin should be avoided in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. This guidance is conveyed in Section 8.6 of product labeling.
L526: ## **Renal impairment:**
L527: No dose modification is recommended in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. No formal clinical trial has been conducted to assess the effect of renal function on the PK of tisotumab vedotin. The effect of mild and moderate renal impairment on tisotumab vedotin and unconjugated MMAE exposure was assessed using the population PK model. The population PK analysis included patients with mild (n=142) and moderate (n=42) renal impairment, in addition to patients with normal (n=215) renal function. There was no marked difference in model-predicted Cycle 1 tisotumab vedotin and unconjugated MMAE exposures between patients with normal renal function and those with mild and moderate renal impairment (Figure 5).

L535: ## Tissue factor (TF) expression and treatment response
L536: Membrane and cytoplasm-associated TF expression was retrospectively tested by IHC on pre-treatment fresh or archival tumor biopsies from 87% (88/101) of patients enrolled in the GCT1015-04 study. IHC results were scored as percent of positive tumor cells (0-100%), and as a TF histology-score (H-score), which was calculated as described in Hong et al., 2020 (PMID: 31796521). Results from 80 samples were considered evaluable by the Applicant and were used for an exploratory analyses of TF expression and treatment response with tisotumab vedotin.
L537: Of the 80 evaluable biopsies, 66 (82.5%) were obtained prior to last systemic therapy and 14 (17.5%) after their last systemic therapy. Approximately 96% (77/80) had detectable membrane TF expression. Median membrane H-score was 120 (range: 0-265) and median percentage of tumor cells positive for membrane TF expression was 60% (range: 0-100%). In the subgroup of patients who were evaluable for tumor response (N=76), confirmed objective responses (CR=6 and PR=14 by IRC) were observed over a wide range of membrane H-scores (Figure 6), including a partial response in a patient whose biopsy had a membrane H-score of 0. Similar results were observed when TF expression was analyzed as percent of tumor cells positive on the membrane or was based on cytoplasm staining. Refer to Section 8.1.4 for additional details and discussion.
L538: **Figure 6: Tumor Membrane H-score at Baseline by Confirmed Best Overall Response as Assessed by IRC, GCT1015-04 study (N=76).**
L539: Source: Applicant's Graph 14.04.01.01.01 – CSR. Whiskers extend to the most extreme observation within 1.5 times the interquartile range from the nearest quartile. Date of cut-off: Feb 06, 2020. Only patients with evaluable TF expression and response are represented.
L540: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L541: No, based on available data from another ADC that contains MMAE, brentuximab vedotin, there is a low potential for clinically relevant interactions between tisotumab vedotin and concomitant medications. Tisotumab vedotin is intended for administration via IV infusion; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L542: ## Data:
L543: ## **Coadministration of CYP3A4 Substrates with Tisotumab Vedotin**
L544: In a clinical DDI study with another MMAE-containing ADC, brentuximab vedotin (1.8 mg/kg), circulating levels of free MMAE (at levels similar to those observed following tisotumab vedotin administration) did not affect the PK of midazolam, a CYP3A4 substrate (Han 2013). Data suggest that circulating concentrations of free MMAE that result from tisotumab vedotin are not likely to cause clinically significant changes in the PK of drugs metabolized by CYP3A4.
L545: ## **Coadministration of CYP3A4 Inhibitors with Tisotumab Vedotin**
L546: Concomitant administration of ketoconazole with brentuximab vedotin led to a 34% increase in free MMAE AUC exposure (Han 2013). By contrast, the PK of brentuximab vedotin were unchanged by concomitant use of ketoconazole. Based on clinical data of brentuximab vedotin,
L547: concomitant use of strong inhibitors of CYP3A4 with tisotumab vedotin may result in a similar increase in free MMAE exposure.
L548: ## Coadministration of CYP3A4 Inducers with Tisotumab Vedotin
L549: Concomitant administration of rifampin with brentuximab vedotin led to a 46% decrease in free MMAE AUC exposure (Han 2013). Brentuximab vedotin PK were unchanged by concomitant use of rifampin. Concomitant use of strong inducers of CYP3A4 such as rifampin with tisotumab vedotin would likely result in similar effects of free MMAE and tisotumab vedotin.
L550: ## The Applicant's Position:
L551: No formal DDI studies have been conducted with tisotumab vedotin. Tisotumab vedotin is an antibody-based therapeutic and DDI involving antibodies are not anticipated. MMAE, the microtubule disrupting agent of tisotumab vedotin, is not expected to produce DDIs via CYP inhibition or induction, and concomitant use of strong inducers or inhibitors of CYP3A4 is not predicted to result in clinically meaningful changes in MMAE exposure.
L552: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L553: The FDA agrees that no tisotumab vedotin dose modifications are recommended in patients who are receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors or inducers. Tisotumab vedotin contains the same linker-payload combination, at the same average drug-to-antibody ratio of 4, as brentuximab vedotin. The geometric mean (CV%) of single dose systemic exposures of unconjugated MMAE at the proposed dosage of tisotumab vedotin [Cmax = 4.73 (51%) ng/mL and AUC0-inf = 39.5 (53%) day.ng/mL, model-predicted in Study 04] are similar to those observed at the approved 1.8 mg/kg Q3W dosage of brentuximab vedotin [Cmax = 4.97(43%) ng/mL and AUC0-inf = 37 (47%) day.ng/mL, per the Adcetris clinical pharmacology review]. Therefore, similar effects of strong CYP3A inhibitors (34% increase in free MMAE AUC) and inducers on unconjugated MMAE PK might be expected with tisotumab vedotin as those observed with brentuximab vedotin. Due to the expected increase in unconjugated MMAE exposure with concomitant use of strong CYP3A inhibitors (which is expected to be similar in magnitude to that in patients with mild HI), patients receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors should be closely monitored for signs of toxicity.
