L571: a. GCT1015-04 safety analysis N=101, efficacy analysis N=101; GEN701 safety analysis N=167, efficacy analysis N=50; GCT1015-03 safety analysis N=4, efficacy analysis N=2; GEN702 safety analysis N=4; SGNTV-001 safety analysis N=56
L572: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L573: The primary evidence of efficacy and safety for tisotumab vedotin monotherapy in subjects with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer is based on the ongoing, open-label, phase 2 GCT1015-04 trial. This study enrolled 101 subjects.
L574: Supportive evidence for efficacy and safety is provided by the expansion cohort of trial GEN701 including extension trial data from 2 subjects with cervical cancer who were subsequently enrolled in GCT1015-03. Additional supportive evidence for safety is provided by GEN702 and an ongoing trial, SGNTV-001, contributing data for analyses of ADA, ECG, and corrected QT interval by Fredericia (QTcF).
L575: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L576: FDA's evaluation of efficacy and safety is primarily based on results from GCT1015-04. GEN701/GCT1015-03 provided some secondary data to support efficacy and safety, and GEN702 provided secondary data to support safety.
L577: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L578: ## 8.1 Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L579: ## 8.1.1 Pivotal Trial GCT1015-04 (innovaTV 204)
L580: ## **Trial Design**
L581: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L582: ## ***Basic Study Design***
L583: This is a single arm, international, multicenter trial of tisotumab vedotin in subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. Eligible subjects had experienced disease progression during or after treatment with a chemotherapy doublet in combination with bevacizumab (if eligible to receive bevacizumab) and had received no more than 2 prior systemic treatment regimens for their recurrent or metastatic disease. Subjects were treated with IV tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W. Imaging was obtained every 6 weeks for the first 30 weeks and then every 12 weeks thereafter, continued until the subject experiences IRC verified disease progression, began a new anti-cancer therapy, withdrew from the trial, or had died, whichever came first. For subjects who achieved a response, a repeat scan was obtained no earlier than 4 weeks (28 days) after the first scan to confirm the response. Survival status was assessed every 60 days (\pm 7 days), beginning from the day of last IMP dose, or more frequently around the time of a database lock. The study schema is shown in Figure 7.
L584: **Figure 7: GCT1015-04 Study Schema**
L585: a: Administration of tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg on day 1 of each cycle. Each treatment cycle is 3 weeks (Q3W).
L586: b: CT or MRI scan every 6 weeks (\pm 7 days) for the first 30 weeks and every 12 weeks (\pm 7 days) thereafter.
L587: c: Optional
L588: d: Survival follow-up should be performed every 60 days (\pm 7 days) or more frequently around the time of database lock.
L589: ## Trial Location
L590: This trial was conducted across 36 sites in the EU and the United States.
L591: ## Choice of Control Group
L592: This was an open-label, single-arm trial with no control group.
L593: ## Diagnostic Criteria and Main Eligibility Criteria
L594: Main inclusion criteria included subjects 18 years of age or older with extra-pelvic metastatic or recurrent cervical cancer of squamous cell, adenocarcinoma, or adeno-squamous histology with disease progression during or after treatment with a doublet chemotherapeutic regimen of paclitaxel + cisplatin/carboplatin or paclitaxel + topotecan, in combination with bevacizumab (if eligible), and ECOG performance status of 0 or 1. Subjects must have received no more than 2 prior lines of recurrent or metastatic cancer therapy. Key exclusion criteria included the presence of baseline neuropathy \ge grade 2 and presence of baseline ocular surface disease. Complete inclusion and exclusion criteria are provided in the protocol and CSR.
L595: ## Dose Selection
L596: The RP2D was identified as 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W in GEN701.
L597: ## Study Treatments
L598: Tisotumab vedotin was administered as an IV infusion at 2.0 mg/kg over a minimum of 30 minutes on day 1 of each treatment cycle (one treatment cycle is 21 days). Each subject's dose was calculated based on the subject's weight rounded to the nearest kilogram, ie,
L599: 2.0 mg/kg × body weight in kg. For subjects who weigh &gt;100 kg, the calculation of IMP dose was normalized to 100 kg (ie, 2.0 mg/kg × 100 kg = 200 mg).
L600: ## **Blinding**
L601: This is a single-arm, open-label trial and therefore there was no blinding.
L602: ## **Administrative Structure**
L603: A DMC was to be consulted on an ad hoc basis in the event of any new significant safety signal, or for significant changes in the established safety profile for tisotumab vedotin. No DMC meetings were held.
L604: ## **Procedures and Schedule**
L605: A list of all assessments and visits is provided in Table 1-1 of the GCT1015-04 protocol and timing of PK samplings in Table 1-2. CT or MRI scans were performed every 6 weeks (\pm 7 days) for the first 30 weeks and every 12 weeks (\pm 7 days) thereafter, calculated from the date of first IMP dosing. All subjects were required to have a baseline ophthalmological evaluation, during screening. Subject were referred to an ophthalmologist for prompt review (preferably within 72 hours and no later than within 1 week) if ocular symptoms arose at any time during trial and/or abnormal findings were noted during the eye examination performed by the treating physician. PK assessments were performed on Days 1, 4 (\pm 1 day), and 8 (\pm 2 days) during Cycle 1 and on Day 1 (\pm 3 days) of Cycle 2. Unscheduled assessments could be performed to mitigate AEs throughout the study.
L606: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L607: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study protocol and has the following additions/clarifications:
L608: - Regarding eligibility criteria:Patients could not be candidates for curative treatment of any kind, including pelvic exenteration surgery or radiation.
- Patients must have had measurable disease as per RECIST v1.1 as determined by the IRC.
- For determination of study eligibility, neoadjuvant or adjuvant chemotherapy or chemotherapy received in combination with radiation therapy did not count as a prior line of systemic treatment.
L609: ## **Study Endpoints**
L610: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L611: The primary endpoint was confirmed ORR based upon RECIST v1.1, assessed by the IRC. Secondary efficacy endpoints included confirmed ORR assessed by investigator, DOR assessed by IRC and investigator, TTR assessed by IRC and investigator, PFS assessed by IRC and investigator, and OS. Other secondary endpoints included AEs and safety laboratory
L612: parameters, PK, and ADAs of tisotumab vedotin.
L613: Exploratory endpoints included:
L614: - TF expression in pre-treatment and post-progression tumor biopsies, circulating TF, proteomic analyses, and genetic variations
- Circulating TF and proteomic analyses
- EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EORTC-QLQ-CX24
L615: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L616: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of study endpoints.
L617: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L618: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L619: The current version of the statistical analysis plan (SAP), version 7.0, was finalized on 17 Jun 2019, prior to the database lock. There were no changes to the analysis of the primary endpoint (confirmed ORR per IRC). The trial was designed such that by applying a one-sided exact binomial test at the 0.025 significance level, the planned sample size of 100 subjects provided \ge 80\% power to reject an ORR of 11%, the combined ORR for available second-line therapies, or less for true ORRs in the range 21% - 25%, representing clinically relevant improvements over available second-line treatments.
L620: Key analysis populations defined include:
L621: - The Full Analysis Set (FAS) and Safety Set, which included all subjects who received at least one dose of tisotumab vedotin. Since the FAS and the Safety set are identical, all safety analyses used the term FAS as the analysis population.
L622: Primary analysis on confirmed ORR was performed on the FAS so that any treated subjects with missing information regarding response to treatment were counted as non-responders. ORR including unconfirmed responders was also analyzed, and confirmed ORR was analyzed by subgroup using a forest plot. Further, confirmed ORR as well as confirmed and unconfirmed ORR, as assessed by the investigator, were analyzed and summarized in the same way.
L623: DOR (months) was listed for all subjects in the FAS with a confirmed best overall response of CR or PR and presented graphically as well as summarized using survival analysis methods. Six-month DOR rate (ie, percentage of subjects with DOR \ge 6 months) and 95% CI were summarized using Kaplan-Meier estimates. TTR was summarized in the FAS population restricted to subjects with a confirmed response using descriptive statistics. PFS and OS were analyzed in the FAS population and presented graphically as well as summarized using Kaplan Meier curves and Kaplan-Meier estimates of the median time, the first and third quartiles, along with approximate 95% confidence interval of the median.
L624: ## **Subgroup analyses:**
L625: Subgroup analyses were performed for the primary endpoint of ORR. Prespecified subgroups included:
L626: - Histology (squamous or non-squamous)
L627: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L628: - Prior cisplatin +radiation (Yes or No)
- Prior lines of systemic regimen (1 or 2); systemic regimen refers to systemic regimen administered in the extra-pelvic metastatic or recurrent setting in this document.
- Response to last systemic regimen (Yes or No)
- Prior first line combination of Paclitaxel + Cisplatin/Carboplatin + Bevacizumab or Paclitaxel + Topotecan + Bevacizumab (Yes or No)
- ECOG (0 or 1)
- Age (&lt;65 or ≥65)
- Region (EU or US)
- Site of disease (non-visceral or visceral) [ad hoc subgroup analysis post-DBL]
L629: Interim Analyses: No formal interim analyses were planned.
L630: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L631: FDA does not use inferential procedures (testing procedures, performance goals, type I error control, etc.) to evaluate single-arm trial results. Therefore, FDA efficacy evaluation is based on the estimated magnitude of ORR and adequate duration of response. Additionally, we note that time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable without a comparator arm.

L682: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L683: Overall, the demographics of subjects in GCT1015-04 are representative of the population of subjects with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer.
L684: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L685: FDA agrees with Applicant's summary of demographic and baseline characteristics for patients included in the study population and generally the patient population is representative of those who will receive this treatment post-approval. FDA disagrees with the Applicant's statement that the study population, in which 95% of the patients were white, is completely representative of a US-based population with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. Only a small number of Hispanic or Latino patients were enrolled to this study and only one Black patient was enrolled to this study. This is in contrast to the demographics of
L686: patients with metastatic cervical cancer in the US, where there is an increased incidence of advanced and metastatic cervical cancer in Hispanic or Latino patients and Black patients in the US, as compared to the incidence in White patients. Despite this, there is no evidence from the data submitted, to conclude that the safety or efficacy for tisotumab vedotin differs by race, such that the results cannot be applied to a broader, more diverse, population of cervical cancer patients in the US, post-approval. Therefore, the review team finds the demographics and characteristics of patients enrolled to be acceptable to demonstrate the safety and efficacy of tisotumab vedotin in a broad US population.

L694: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L695: The distribution of histology subtypes in GCT1015-04 was representative of the natural distribution of subtypes in a second-line and later population of patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer (NCI, 2020) and included squamous cell carcinoma (68.3%), adenocarcinoma (26.7%), and adenosquamous carcinoma (5.0%).
L696: GCT1015-04 enrolled 14.9% of subjects in the US. All other subjects were enrolled in Europe. As the treatment guidelines for recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer and ethnic diversity are generally similar in these regions, the trial population is considered representative of the US patient population.
L697: The majority of subjects (69.3%) had previously been treated with bevacizumab in GCT1015-04. This is higher than health claims data indicating that approximately half of all US patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer receive bevacizumab as part of a first-line treatment regimen (Kantar Health 2020). The inclusion of an appropriate number of subjects previously treated with bevacizumab, an expected histologic distribution, and the fact that biomarker (TF) enrichment was not required demonstrates that the GCT1015-04 trial population is representative of the US patient population.

L712: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L713: ## **Data:**
L714: Individual trial results for GCT1015-04 are presented side by side with GEN701/GCT1015-03 in Section 8.1.4. These results are also summarized below.
L715: The primary endpoint, confirmed ORR assessed by IRC per RECIST v1.1, was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9%–33.3%), including 7 (6.9%) CRs and 17 (16.8%) PRs (GCT1015-04 CSR, Table 14.02.01.01.01). The 95% CI excluded the reference ORR of 11% (P=0.0002) (GCT1015-04 clinical study report [CSR] Table 14.02.01.01.01). Disease control rate (DCR) was 72.3%, which included 7 (6.9%) CRs, 17 (16.8%) PRs and 49 subjects (48.5%) with a best overall response of SD. Among the 101 subjects, 77 subjects (76.2%) had target lesion reductions. Excluding 4 subjects, who did not have a postbaseline scan, resulted in 77/97 (79.4%) with a target lesion reduction.
L716: ## **Subgroup Analysis of ORR per IRC**
L717: Response rates were generally consistent across subgroups including region, prior treatment with bevacizumab, and ECOG score, with 95% confidence intervals overlapping with the confirmed ORR of the overall trial population (GCT1015-04 CSR Graph 14.02.01.01).
L718: ## **ORR Assessed by Investigator**
L719: In general, investigator assessments of efficacy were similar to IRC assessments (m2.7.3, Section 3.2.6). Confirmed ORR as assessed by investigator was 20.8% (95% CI: 13.4%, 30.0%), which included 5 CRs (5.0%) and 16 PRs (15.8%) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.01.01.02). DCR was 83.2% (95% CI: 74.4%, 89.9%), which included responders as well as 63 subjects with SD (62.4%). The concordance rate between confirmed response assessments per IRC and responses per investigator was 85.1% (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.05).
L720: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L721: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L722: Treatment with tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W resulted in clinically meaningful and durable responses in subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after doublet chemotherapy with bevacizumab (if eligible). Confirmed ORR of 23.8% was generally consistent across subgroups, with 95% CIs overlapping with the confirmed ORR of the overall trial population. Importantly, confirmed ORR was consistent across histologies, prior vs no prior bevacizumab as part of frontline treatment.
L723: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L724: FDA conducted an independent analysis of ORR. The confirmed ORR assessed by IRC was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9%, 33.3%) out of 101 patients, including 7 (6.9%) CRs and 17 (16.8%) PRs, while the confirmed ORR assessed by investigator was 20.8% (95% CI: 13.4%, 30.0%), including 5 (5.0%) CRs and 16 (15.8%) PRs. Characteristics of patients who experienced responses on GCT1015-04 are shown in Table 14.

L734: Overall, the disease burden in patients experiencing tumor responses was low. Only one patient experiencing a response had tumor sum of diameters &gt;5 cm in diameter, and no patients experiencing a response had bulky metastatic disease. However, based upon the mechanism of action of tisotumab, as an antibody-drug conjugate, there does not seem to be reason to expect that patients with bulkier disease may not respond to therapy with tisotumab vedotin, and the results observed in Study GCT1015-104 may have been due to a small sample size or chance. The review team did not think that restrictions on use of tisotumab vedotin, according to disease burden, were warranted.

L739: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L740: ## Data:
L741: N/A
L742: ## The Applicant's Position:
L743: Steps taken to ensure the accuracy and reliability of data included selection of qualified trial sites and investigators, GCP training of trial personnel, and protocol training before initiation of the trial. In addition, site monitoring visits to review source documents against the eCRF were performed by the contract research organization (CRO), with sponsor oversight of CRO's duties as per standard operating procedure. Investigators were updated on an ongoing basis on trial progress, important trial milestones, and specific protocol specified requirements via newsletters and flash letters. Data collected by external vendors (eg, laboratory results, ECG results etc. excluding central imaging data) were transferred to the sponsor on an ongoing basis and reviewed, together with other data, for accuracy and completeness. Any discrepancies were resolved with the trial site. In addition, prior to locking the database a thorough review was performed to enhance the quality and ensure completeness.
L744: The sponsor was blinded to the IRC data with the exception of a limited number of personnel, according to the protocol-specific blinding plan, who were unblinded when preparing for database lock in order to ensure data quality and completeness of the IRC dataset.
L745: Audits were conducted undertaking a risk-based approach. Five sites (IT03, ES01, US11, US13, and CZ01) were audited. In addition, vendors and central laboratories (b) (6) were audited.
L746: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L747: FDA OSI conducted clinical inspections and did not identify issues with data quality or integrity. Please see Section 4.1 for additional information.
L748: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L749: ## Data
L750: Individual trial results for GCT1015-04 are presented side by side with GEN701/GCT1015-03 in Section 8.1.4 for the secondary endpoints of confirmed ORR assessed by investigator, DOR assessed by IRC and investigator, TTR assessed by IRC and investigator, PFS assessed by IRC and investigator, and OS. These results are summarized below.
L751: ## Duration of Response
L752: Confirmed responses to tisotumab vedotin were durable, with a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 months to NR) as assessed by IRC. An estimated 62.4% of confirmed responders had a DOR of 6 months or longer (95% CI: 36.7%, 80.1%) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.02.01).
L753: ## Time to Response as Assessed by IRC
L754: Responses occurred early in treatment, with a median TTR of 1.4 months (range, 1.1 to 5.1 months) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.02.01). Most responders (19/24, 79.2%) recorded their first radiologic response at the first postbaseline scan.
L755: ## Progression-Free Survival as Assessed by IRC
L756: Seventy-four subjects (73.3%) experienced a PFS event (progression or death), as assessed by IRC. The median PFS was 4.2 months (95% CI: 3.0, 4.4 months). The estimated PFS rate at 6 months was 30.2% (95% CI: 20.8%, 40.1%) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.03.01).
L757: ## Overall Survival
L758: As of the data cutoff date, 58 subjects (57.4%) had died. Median OS was 12.1 months (95% CI: 9.6, 13.9 months) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.02.04).
L759: ## The Applicant's Position:
L760: Responses to tisotumab vedotin were durable, with a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI 4.2 months- NR) as assessed by IRC. An estimated 62% subjects had a DOR of 6 months or longer. Responses occurred early in treatment, with a median TTR by IRC of 1.4 months (range, 1.1 to 5.1 months). Median PFS by IRC was 4.2 months (95% CI 3.0 to 4.4 months) and the estimated PFS rate at 6 months was 30% (95% CI 20.8% to 40.1%). PFS data are supportive of ORR and DOR data. The median OS was 12.1 months (95% CI 9.6 to 13.9 months). OS data are supportive of the ORR and DOR data.
L761: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L762: FDA conducted an independent analysis of ORR and DOR. Among the 24 confirmed responders, 10 had progressed and 14 were still in response at the data cutoff date of February 6, 2020. The median DOR was 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 month, NR), and the DOR was ranged from 1.4 months to 11.0 months. An estimated 62.4% of confirmed responders had a DOR of 6 months or longer (95% CI: 36.7%, 80.1%).
L763: Time-to-event endpoints such as time-to-response, PFS, and OS are not interpretable for single-
L764: arm studies and do not factor into the benefit/risk determination for this application.

L786: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L787: ## **Data:**
L788: In the single arm trial GCT1015-04, HRQOL was assessed by collection of data from 2 validated questionnaires completed by trial subjects at screening visit (baseline), Cycle 2 (D1), Cycle 4 (D1), and treatment discontinuation; EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EORTC-QLQ-CX24.
L789: The compliance and completion rates of EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EORTC-QLQ-CX24 at screening visit (baseline), Cycle 2 (D1), Cycle 4 (D1), and treatment discontinuation were reported (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.04.03.02).
L790: In terms of HRQOL findings, in general, scores in most EORTC-QLQ-C30 domains numerically declined between the screening visit (baseline) and Cycle 4 (D1), as well as treatment
L791: discontinuation (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.04.03.01). Scores in most EORTC-QLQ-CX24 domains generally remained stable between the screening visit (baseline) and Cycle 4 (D1) (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.04.04.01).
L792: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L793: HRQOL was in general maintained or showed small but not clinically meaningful decreases on most domains between baseline and Cycle 4. The larger decreases in some domains/items at time of treatment discontinuation are expected as most subjects at the timepoint experienced progressive disease or discontinuation of trial drug due to ongoing AEs.
L794: Given the single-arm trial design, limited data collected, lack of published data on HRQOL in cervical cancer, the clinical meaningfulness of these declines is hard to interpret.
L795: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L796: FDA agrees with the Applicant that it is difficult to interpret PROs, given the lack of a control arm and the small sample size.

L821: Tisotumab vedotin resulted in clinically meaningful confirmed ORR per IRC of 23.8% (95% CI 15.9% - 33.3%) in subjects with previously treated recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. L824: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L825: ## Data:
L826: ## DOR and TTR by IRC:
L827: Confirmed responses to tisotumab vedotin were durable, with a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI 4.2 months – NR) in GCT1015-04 and 5.1 months (95% CI 3.0 months – 9.7 months) in GEN701/GCT1015-03 (Table 17 and Figure 9). Reasons for the observed difference in median DOR across trials remain to be elucidated; however, it is noted that more comprehensive safety management guidelines may have enabled subjects to stay on therapy longer in GCT1015-04, and that GCT1015-04 also enrolled a population with generally better prognostic factors. An estimated 62% of confirmed responders had a DOR of 6 months or longer in GCT1015-04.
L828: Responses occurred early in treatment, with a median TTR of 1.4 months in GCT1015-04 and 2.8 months in GEN701/GCT1015-03 (Table 17 and Figure 10).

L858: ## The Applicant's Position:
L859: Responses to tisotumab vedotin were durable, with a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI 4.2 months- NR) as assessed by IRC. An estimated 62.4% subjects had a DOR of 6 months or longer. Responses occurred early in treatment, with a median TTR by IRC of 1.4 months (range, 1.1 to 5.1 months). Median PFS by IRC was 4.2 months (95% CI 3.0 to 4.4 months) and the estimated PFS rate at 6 months was 30.2% (95% CI 20.8% to 40.1%). PFS data are supportive of ORR and DOR data. The median OS was 12.1 months (95% CI 9.6 to 13.9 months). OS data are supportive of ORR and DOR data.
L860: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L861: As previously stated, FDA's primary efficacy assessment is based on results from GCT1015-04. Please refer to Section 8.1.2 for FDA's assessment of DOR on GCT1015-04. FDA did not replicate the Applicant's analyses from GEN701/GCT-1015-03 due to the small sample size.
L862: FDA does not consider PFS and OS data interpretable due to the lack of a comparator arm in a single arm trial.
L863: ## Subpopulations
L864: ## Data:
L865: In both GCT1015-04 and GEN701/GCT1015-03, confirmed ORR were generally consistent across subgroups including region, prior treatment with bevacizumab, and ECOG score, with 95% CIs overlapping with the confirmed ORR of the overall trial population (Figure 13).
L866: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761208}
## {tisotumab vedotin}
L867: While GEN701/GCT1015-03 enrolled a higher percentage of subjects with adenocarcinoma or adenosquamous histologies compared to GCT1015-04, ORRs were similar in squamous and non-squamous histologies across the trials. These data indicate that tisotumab vedotin treatment provides clinical benefit to a broad population regardless of histology subtype.
L868: While no confirmed responses were observed in either trial for the subgroup of subjects who were age 65 years or older, the number of subjects in this subgroup was small and there is a wide confidence interval that overlaps that of the overall trial population. At this time, there is no biological rationale suggesting that treatment with tisotumab vedotin would be less efficacious in subjects over 65 years of age.

L873: ## **Tissue Factor Expression and Objective Response:**
L874: Biomarker-based subject selection was not required in GCT1015-04. Tumor TF expression was retrospectively assessed using a validated immunohistochemistry (IHC) assay in tumor biopsies from subjects with second line or greater recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. TF expression was evaluable in 80/100 (80%) of tumor biopsies collected, and the majority of these were archival and collected prior to last systemic therapy (82.5% [66/80]).
L875: Membrane TF expression (\ge 1\% of tumor cells with TF staining) was observed in 96% of subjects with an evaluable biopsy. Responses to tisotumab vedotin were observed over a wide range of membrane TF expression levels in GCT1015-04, and comparable distribution of TF expression was observed in the IRC-assessed confirmed best overall response categories (m5.3.5.2/GCT1015-04/sec11.11.1). These data suggest that tisotumab vedotin (2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W) is a potential treatment option for patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after chemotherapy, regardless of TF expression levels via IHC.
L876: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L877: Results of subgroup analyses were consistent across trials, with no meaningful differences in confirmed ORR observed for any of the subgroups assessed. The population for efficacy analyses was an all-comers population; that is, TF expression was not required for enrollment. In GCT1015-04, TF was expressed in the majority of subjects with cervical cancer tumors (96.3%) and responses were observed regardless of TF expression levels.
L878: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L879: ## **Subgroup Analyses**
L880: FDA considers all subgroup analyses of ORR exploratory. In general, these analyses were supportive of primary ORR results. However, none of the 13 patients \ge 65 years enrolled to
L881: GCT1015-04 experienced a tumor response. The reasons for this finding are unclear and the ability to draw conclusions is limited by the small sample size of patients over 65. It is possible that the tisotumab vedotin may be less tolerable to patients \ge 65 years of age, and this could negatively impact the chance for efficacy.
L882: FDA also evaluated ORR by IRC in additional subgroups in GCT1015-04 as shown in Table 21 and these analyses were supportive of primary ORR results.

L885: ## Prior Bevacizumab
L886: The Applicant's subgroup analyses included an analysis of patients who received/did not receive bevacizumab (Figure 13). FDA reviewed these data and also did a subgroup analysis of patients who received/did not receive bevacizumab in combination with a chemotherapy doublet as first-line systemic treatment (Table 21). In both analyses, which include a small number of patients and are exploratory, ORR was higher in patients who had not received prior bevacizumab. The reasons for this are unclear.
L887: ## Tissue Factor
L888: To assess tumor TF expression, the Applicant considered both membrane TF expression and cytoplasm TF expression as % of cells staining positive and also calculated membrane TF H-
L889: score and cytoplasm TF H-score. Cytoplasmic and membrane tissue were first graded for TF expression intensity (0=absent, 1=low, 2=intermediate, and 3=high). Then, a separate H-score was calculated for cytoplasm and for membrane for each evaluable tumor sample. H-scores for cytoplasmic and membrane TF expression were calculated using this equation: H-score= [ 1x (%cells with 1+) ] + [ 2 x (%cells with 2+) ] + [ 3 x (%cells with 3+) ] .
L890: FDA assessed baseline tissue factor (TF) expression in patients enrolled to GCT1015-04. Out of 101 patients, 100 patients had biopsy tissue available. Out of the 100 patients with biopsies, 88 patients had tumor samples evaluated for TF expression with data reported, however the Applicant only considered 80 patients to have tumor samples evaluable for TF expression. This was clarified in an information request to the Applicant sent on April 21, 2021 in which the Applicant stated that there were issues with the quality of IHC for 8 of the 88 samples.
L891: Of the 80 patients with assessable tumor samples, there was one patient with 0% membrane and cytoplasm TF positive tumor who experienced a tumor response and 4 patients not evaluable for response. The Applicant additionally conducted analyses examining tumor response by TF expression as characterized by membrane and cytoplasm H-scores. The Applicant did not find an association between TF expression and tumor response and FDA agrees with this assessment, although noting that data are available from a relatively small number of patients (n=76). Refer to Applicant and FDA assessments in Section 6.3.2 for more information.
L892: Based on the data available, a companion diagnostic is not needed to select patients for treatment with tisotumab vedotin.
L893: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L894: The FDA's Assessment:
No additional considerations.
L895: ## 8.1.5 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L896: ## Data:
L897: Results are based on a median follow-up time of 10.0 and 4.0 months in GCT1015-04 and GEN701/GCT1015-03, respectively. The follow-up time is longer in GCT1015-04 as long-term survival data were not collected in GEN701/GCT1015-03. The GCT1015-04 primary analysis data cutoff ensured that all responders were followed for a minimum of 6 months. Efficacy in GCT1015-04 as well as GEN701 are described in Section 8.1.4 demonstrating consistency of results in terms of ORR by IRC and PFS. The median OS was 12.1 months (95% CI 9.6-13.9 mos) for subjects treated in GCT1015-04 and was not measured in GEN701.
L898: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L899: In pivotal trial GCT1015-04 (n=101), treatment with tisotumab vedotin resulted in a confirmed ORR of 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9%; 33.3%) by IRC assessment. Responses included 6.9% of subjects with a CR, 16.8% with a PR, and 48.5% with SD, for a DCR of 72.3%. Confirmed responses to tisotumab vedotin were durable, with a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2; NR) as assessed by IRC and an estimated 62.4% of confirmed responders having a DOR of 6 months or longer in GCT1015-04.
L900: Responses occurred early in treatment and were generally consistent across subgroups, with 95% confidence intervals overlapping with the confirmed ORR of the overall trial population. These results are supported by data from the GEN701 trial.
L901: Benefit was consistently observed across subgroups including histology, ECOG score, region, and in subjects with or without prior treatment with bevacizumab. In addition, responses to tisotumab vedotin were observed regardless of TF expression level in tumor biopsies.
L902: Favorable response data for tisotumab vedotin are further supported by encouraging PFS and OS data. In GCT1015-04, the median PFS by IRC was 4.2 months (range: 0.0+ - 15.1+ months) and median OS was 12.1 months (range: 0.7-17.9+ months).
L903: Overall, the data demonstrate that treatment with tisotumab vedotin resulted in clinically meaningful efficacy in subjects with previously treated recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer, a serious and life-threatening disease with a high unmet need.
L904: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L905: FDA's conclusions regarding efficacy are primarily based on results from GCT1015-04, a multicenter, single arm trial. FDA did not replicate the Applicant's integrated assessment of efficacy including study GEN701/GCT1015-03 as noted in previous sections. For Study GCT1015-04, the IRC-confirmed ORR in 101 patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after chemotherapy was 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9, 33.3). Median DOR was 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2, NR). ORR results were generally consistent across subgroups, with the exception of patients \ge 65 years old. No patients in this age group experienced a tumor response, however FDA notes that only 13 patients \ge65 years were enrolled to the trial. Additionally, 63% of patients enrolled on GCT1015-04 had received prior bevacizumab, including 13 of the 24 responders (54%) to tisotumab vedotin. Since doublet chemotherapy in combination with bevacizumab is considered to be the standard of care regimen for first-line metastatic cervical cancer in the US, this factor indicates that the study population enrolled on GCT1015-04 is overall reflective of the general population of patients with cervical cancer in the US. Finally, FDA did not consider PFS and OS results in the efficacy assessment as these time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable due to the lack of a comparator arm in a single-arm trial.
L906: Patients with advanced cervical cancer who progress following chemotherapy in the recurrent or metastatic setting represent an area of unmet medical need and treatment options are
L907: limited to single agent chemotherapies, with response rates generally ranging 5-21%. As mentioned, the majority of patients enrolled to GCT1015-04 had received prior treatment with a chemotherapy doublet with bevacizumab. Data on the efficacy of commonly used single agent chemotherapies in the post-bevacizumab setting is lacking but is likely to be similar to or worse than the efficacy of the same agents in patients who had not received prior bevacizumab in the same setting. Given the data from GCT1015-04, and considering the current therapeutic landscape of metastatic cervical cancer, FDA considers the ORR of 24% with a median DOR of 8.3 months to be an improvement over available therapies. Therefore, the review team concludes that these results are reasonably likely to predict the clinical benefit of tisotumab in patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer following chemotherapy and recommends the current BLA for accelerated approval Confirmation of clinical benefit will be based on results from the ongoing randomized clinical trial, innovaTV 301, which is comparing tisotumab vedotin to investigator's choice chemotherapy.
L908: ## 8.2 Review of Safety
L909: ## 8.2.1 Safety Review Approach
L910: ## **Data:**
L911: The safety profile summarized in this application consists of data from 4 clinical trials of tisotumab vedotin monotherapy including 272 unique subjects. All subjects received the recommended starting dose of 2.0 mg/kg of tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W. The number of subjects in each of the 4 safety analysis groups is described below.
L912: The data from these trials are grouped into 4 safety analysis sets:
L913: - The GCT1015-04 group includes the 101 subjects with cervical cancer from the pivotal trial.
- Pool 1 (Supportive Cervical) includes 57 subjects with cervical cancer tumor types from the GEN701 Cohort Expansion part, GEN702 Cohort Expansion part, and GCT1015-03 trials. This pool evaluates safety in subjects with cervical cancer included in trials of tisotumab vedotin other than GCT1015-04.
- Pool 2 (All Cervical) combines GCT1015-04 with Pool 1 for a total of 158 subjects with cervical cancer.
- Pool 3 (All Tumor Types) adds the 114 subjects with non-cervical tumor types from GEN701 and GEN702 to the All Cervical cohort for a total of 272 subjects. This population is intended to represent the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin in the largest possible group of subjects who received the RP2D. Inclusion of Pool 3 allows assessments of safety in subjects with a diverse set of tumor types and increases the total number of subjects.
L914: The primary evidence of safety is derived from the GCT1015-04 data as the subjects represent a more homogeneous patient population than the pooled data. In addition, all subjects in GCT1015-04 received the same protocol-specified mitigation measures and dose modification
L915: requirements for safety. Other key features in this pivotal trial compared to the pooled analysis data sets that incorporate data from other trials include:
L916: - Limitations in the number of prior therapies received (no more than 2 prior lines)
- Exclusion of subjects who were refractory to their primary therapies and/or did not receive a first-line standard-of-care therapy for recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer
- Treatment of all subjects with comprehensive and current protocol-defined AE management guidelines, including the most up to date recommended dose modifications for ocular AEs and peripheral neuropathy.
L917: Thus, the pivotal trial data from GCT1015-04 represent the core safety population relevant to the proposed indication.
L918: Safety data from GCT1015-04 are presented side-by-side in tabular format with the 3 pooled safety analysis sets. Safety data for each individual trial are presented in their respective individual CSRs in (m5.3.5).
L919: Additional SAE data from the Global Safety Database for ongoing trials GCT1015-04 and SGNTV-001 from the CSR data cutoff dates through 16 August 2020 are provided with the respective CSRs in (m5.3.5.2) as SAE listings. Cumulative SAE listings from the Global Safety Database for ongoing trials GCT1015-05, GCT1015-06, and SGNTV-002 through 16 August 2020 are included in (m5.3.5.4). Safety data including ECG and QTcF results for SGNTV-001 through 22 August 2019 are presented in an abbreviated CSR (m5.3.5.2 SGNTV-001 CSR) and immunogenicity data (ie, ADA) from this trial also contribute to a combined population ADA analysis.
L920: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L921: A comprehensive review of safety data pertinent to tisotumab vedotin was conducted across 4 clinical studies including subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after doublet chemotherapy with bevacizumab as well as other tumor types. Events of interest were well-characterized based on a thorough analysis of all available safety data in subjects, including by prior radiotherapy, bevacizumab, and prior lines of systemic regimen.
L922: The safety data were consistent across the pooled analyses and demonstrate an acceptable safety profile of tisotumab vedotin monotherapy for the treatment of patients with recurrent and metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after chemotherapy.
L923: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L924: FDA's safety analysis is primarily based on results from GCT1015-04, a single arm trial of tisotumab vedotin in 101 patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after chemotherapy. One reason for this is that the GCT1015-04 protocol contains the most up-to-date risk mitigation strategies and dose modification strategies for tisotumab vedotin. To assess adverse events of special interest (AESIs), FDA additionally reviewed data from the Pool 2 population which included a total of 158 patients with cervical cancer treated at the recommended dose of tisotumab vedotin across trials. FDA chose this population to assess AESIs in a slightly larger population of patients with cervical cancer. To
L925: assess very infrequent safety events, FDA evaluated data from the Pool 3 population which included patients with other tumor types treated at the recommended dose of tisotumab vedotin across trials (n=272). FDA did not review all safety data submitted from these other trials. In the sections below, FDA notes which data were used to support safety conclusions.
L926: FDA's safety analysis focused on deaths, and treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) including serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modifications including discontinuations, grade \ge 3 TEAEs, and AESIs. AESIs for tisotumab vedotin include ocular toxicity, peripheral neuropathy, hemorrhage, infusion-related reactions, and pneumonitis.
L927: The safety results in this section are based on data submitted with an initial data cutoff date of February 6, 2020. FDA additionally reviewed safety data from the 90-day safety update with a data cutoff date of September 25, 2020. The safety update did not include new safety signals; other key findings from the safety update are discussed in the appropriate sections below. Refer to Section 8.2.10 for FDA's overall safety assessment.
L928: ## Overall Exposure
L929: ## Data:
L930: A total of 272 subjects have received at least 1 dose of tisotumab vedotin. All subjects received the recommended dose of 2.0 mg/kg of tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W. The number of subjects in each safety analysis group is provided in Table 22.

L934: GEN701, GEN702, and GCT1015-03). In the patient populations included in these analyses the dose administered was 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, meaning the maximum dose per infusion was capped at 200 mg tisotumab vedotin for the GCT1015-04 trial. For trials that implemented a cap based on BMI, dose was capped at the weight that, combined with the subject's height, would lead to a BMI of 30 kg/m2.

L940: Overall exposure is adequate to support the characterization of the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin. Median duration of treatment with tisotumab vedotin in the GCT1015-04 pivotal trial was 4.24 months (range 0.7 to 16.0 months) and median relative dose intensity was 95.9% (range, 44% to 114%). Relative dose intensity was consistent across groups (&gt;90%) and showed that subjects generally received the drug as planned across safety analysis groups.
L941: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L942: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of exposure to tisotumab vedotin on GCT1015-04. FDA did not independently verify exposure to tisotumab vedotin in the other safety populations. FDA agrees that exposure is adequate to characterize safety of tisotumab vedotin.

L954: ## The Applicant's Position:
L955: Demographic and baseline characteristics of the subjects in GCT1015-04 are generally representative of patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who have received at least one prior systemic therapy. The demographic and baseline characteristics were similar among cervical patients across studies as indicated by Pools 1 and 2. With the exception of having more subjects over 65 years of age in Pool 3, the pooled safety analysis sets had no notable differences in demographics.
L956: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L957: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that the GCT1015-04 population is generally representative of a US-based patient with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with respect to disease and treatment characteristics. However, FDA notes that a relatively small number of Hispanic or Latino patients were enrolled, and only one Black patient was enrolled to GCT1015-04. This contrasts with the fact that there is an increased incidence of cervical cancer,
L958: particularly advanced/ metastatic disease, in Hispanic or Latino patients and Black patients, as compared to white patients in the US. Despite that the study did not enroll a racially diverse population, the review team still determined that the results, based upon other important disease characteristics and prior therapies, are applicable to the cervical cancer population treated in the US, and supportive of FDA approval.
L959: Otherwise, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description and assessment of the safety population in GCT1015-04. FDA notes that Pool 3 includes a larger proportion of patients aged 65 and older compared to the GCT1015-04 population.
L960: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L961: ## Data:
L962: The sponsor is providing safety data from a total of 272 subjects who received at least 1 dose of tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W. The GCT1015-04 pivotal trial evaluated tisotumab vedotin monotherapy in subjects with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. All 101 subjects in this GCT1015-04 safety analysis set received tisotumab vedotin at the recommended dose of 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W.
L963: ## The Applicant's Position:
L964: The safety database is adequate for an informed assessment of the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin monotherapy in subjects with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. Additional subjects with other tumor types were included in supportive pooled analyses. The overall incidence of TEAEs was similar across the safety analysis groups and the events observed are considered to be representative of the overall safety profile.
L965: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L966: FDA agrees with the Applicant that the safety database is adequate for assessment of the tisotumab vedotin safety profile.
L967: ## 8.2.2 Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L968: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L969: ## Data:
L970: Steps taken to ensure the accuracy and reliability of data in the GCT1015-04, GEN701, GEN702, and GCT1015-03 trials included selection of qualified trial sites and investigators, GCP training of trial personnel, and protocol training before initiation of the trial. In addition, site monitoring visits to review source documents against the eCRF were performed by the CRO for each trial, with sponsor oversight of the CRO's duties as per standard operating procedure. Investigators were updated on an ongoing basis on trial progress, important trial milestones, and specific protocol specified requirements via newsletters and flash letters. Data collected by external vendors (eg, laboratory results, ECG results etc. excluding central imaging data) were transferred to the sponsor on an ongoing basis and reviewed, together with other data, for
L971: accuracy and completeness. Any discrepancies were resolved with the trial site. The sponsor was blinded to IRC data with the exception of a limited number of personnel, according to the protocol-specific blinding plan, who were unblinded when preparing for database lock in order to ensure data quality and completeness of the IRC dataset.
L972: Prior to locking the database in the GCT1015-04 trial, a thorough review was performed to enhance the quality and ensure completeness. The database for the Expansion Part of the GEN701 trial was locked 20-Jan-2020. The database was reopened and relocked on 26-Mar-2020 for inclusion of corrected data on ADAs. The database was reopened and relocked on 25-Nov-2020 based on a data review that resulted in data queries to sites and subsequent data changes.
L973: Site audits were conducted in all trials as outlined in the respective CSRs.
L974: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L975: No issues were identified regarding data integrity or submission quality that had an effect on the safety review.
L976: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L977: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of data integrity based on our own review and clinical inspections from the FDA Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI). Please see Section 4.1 for additional information.
L978: ## **Categorization of Adverse Event**
L979: ## **Data:**
L980: N/A
L981: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L982: Safety was assessed by the surveillance and recording of AEs (including SAEs and AEs of special interest [AESIs]), clinical laboratory test results, ECGs, ophthalmology examinations, and vital signs.
L983: ## **Adverse Events:**
L984: A treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) is defined as a newly occurring or worsening AE that is reported on or after the first dose of trial drug and with onset date on or before 30 days after the last dose of trial drug. TEAEs are coded to standard preferred terms and system organ classifications (SOCs) using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA, Version 22.1) for all trials at the integrated summary of safety (ISS) level. All AEs, including SAEs, are considered TEAEs unless otherwise specified. TEAEs that were associated with disease progression were excluded from the safety analysis (Preferred terms of disease progression, malignant neoplasm progression, metastases to CNS, oesophageal cancer metastatic, and pericardial effusion malignant). In the event that the frequencies for multiple preferred terms were numerically equal, the preferred terms were presented in alphabetical order. Sort order was determined by the GCT1015-04 column.
L985: Treatment-related TEAEs refer to events that were assessed by the investigator as "possibly
L986: related” or “related” to tisotumab vedotin. If a subject had multiple events occurring in the same SOC or preferred term, it was counted as a related event if at least 1 of the occurrences was considered to be “possibly related” or “related” to tisotumab vedotin.
L987: TEAEs were investigator-assessed for severity grading and seriousness. If an event changed in National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for AEs (NCI CTCAE) grade or severity, the event with the worst/highest severity was counted. Missing NCI CTCAE grade was tabulated as “missing.”
L988: TEAE grades are based on the NCI CTCAE grades as reported in each trial. NCI CTCAE version 4.03 was used in GEN701, GEN702, GCT1015-03 and version 5.0 was used in GCT1015-04. In TEAE summary tables, a subject was counted only once for each preferred term at maximum grade.
L989: ## **Adverse Events of Special Interest:**
L990: AESIs have been identified for tisotumab vedotin based on potential or theoretical risk to patients from the mechanism of action of tisotumab vedotin, known biology or tissue factor, and observed findings in clinical trial data. Three AESIs have been identified: ocular AEs, peripheral neuropathy, and bleeding AEs.
L991: Search criteria for these AESIs are based on standardized MedDRA queries (SMQs), whenever possible, or the most relevant level of the MedDRA hierarchy that most suitably and completely characterizes the event at issue.
L992: ## **Ophthalmological Examinations:**
L993: Baseline ophthalmology examinations were to be performed by qualified ophthalmologists during screening. Such examinations were implemented from the start in pivotal trial GCT1015-04 and were incorporated later through amendments to other trials, eg, GEN701 Protocol Amendment 12 (22 December 2016). In addition, subjects were to be referred for additional ophthalmological examinations if ocular symptoms occurred or in the case of objective findings from scheduled eye exams at the site, if performed.
L994: In addition to baseline and unscheduled exams, some trials included prespecified ophthalmological exams on trial. These included GEN701 (exams on Day 8 of every other cycle and at end of trial) and GEN702 (exams in Cycles 1 to 9 and at end of treatment). Assessments performed included visual acuity, Schirmer's test, slit lamp, inspections of the conjunctivas and corneas including staining, intraocular pressure, and fundoscopy. Further details on ophthalmological exams are provided in the trial protocols.
L995: ## **Electrocardiograms:**
L996: ECG variables were summarized using mean, standard deviation (STD), minimum, maximum, and median for each analysis group at 2 time points: baseline and end of treatment or safety follow up visit, including changes from baseline.
L997: Central reviewer assessed the 12 lead ECG to summarize the number and percentage of subjects with normal, not clinically significant abnormal, and clinically significant abnormal
L998: results for each time point. Calculation of QT, PR, RR, and QRS intervals was conducted in trials GCT1015-04 and SGNTV001. Further details on the ECGs can be found in the trial protocols.
L999: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1000: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of adverse events on GCT1015-04. TEAEs were defined as AEs occurring after the first dose of tisotumab vedotin to within 30 days after the last dose. FDA's safety assessment focused on the frequency and severity of all TEAEs with particular focus on TEAEs leading to death, SAEs, grade \ge 3 TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modifications, and the three AESIs identified by the Applicant: ocular toxicity, peripheral neuropathy, and bleeding/hemorrhage. FDA also considered pneumonitis an AESI given reports of pneumonitis with other "vedotin" containing products.
L1001: FDA did not consider treatment-related AEs in the safety analysis, as attribution is subjective and challenging.
L1002: ## **Data:**
L1003: N/A.
L1004: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1005: Baseline values for clinical laboratory tests are defined as the last measurements taken before initial trial drug administration. A comprehensive list of laboratory parameters collected in each trial can be found in the CSRs.
L1006: The following analyses were performed on the 4 safety analysis groups as part of the integrated safety analysis:
L1007: - Incidence of potentially drug-induced liver laboratory abnormalities (aspartate transaminase (AST)/alanine transaminase (ALT) &gt;3\timesupper limit of normal (ULN) + Total Bilirubin &gt;2\timesULN)
- CTCAE grade shift from baseline to highest post-baseline for serum chemistry, hematology, and coagulation
L1008: Laboratory results were graded using NCI CTCAE (version 4.03 for GEN701 and GEN702, and version 5.0 for GCT1015-04).
L1009: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1010: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of laboratory assessments on GCT1015-04.
L1011: ## 8.2.3 Safety Results
L1012: ## Deaths
L1013: ## **Data:**
L1014: TEAEs leading to death (ie, Grade 5 TEAEs) were reported in 4 subjects (4.0%) in GCT1015-04
L1015: (Table 26). Deaths were due to events of septic shock, ileus, and death due to unknown causes in 2 subjects, 1 of which is represented in Table 26 with a cause of death of “unknown” and 1 of which is represented as “other”). Both events of death due to unknown causes were reported as “death” and arose from subjects who died in a home care setting; no autopsy was performed. The event of fatal ileus was secondary to clinical disease progression and the subject died during surgical operation for this event; thus this death was considered due to deterioration of underlying cancer by the investigator. One additional grade 5 TEAE of general physical health deterioration was reported in Pool 3 (ISS Table 10.2.9).
L1016: The only death considered possibly related to tisotumab vedotin in GCT1015-04 was due to septic shock. The death involved a 64-year-old female with cervical cancer and metastases to the lung, liver and lymph nodes and no relevant medical history who was admitted to intensive care 10 days after the first dose of tisotumab vedotin with fever, general malaise, and grade 4 neutropenic sepsis. During hospitalization, the subject’s neutrophil count spontaneously improved and the event of neutropenic sepsis resolved (day 15). However, the subject was diagnosed with infectious colitis (day 12) and developed septic shock (day 17) 1 day after a colonoscopy with biopsy was performed. The subject died on the 8th day of hospitalization (day 20) despite supportive treatment with antibiotics. No autopsy was performed.
L1017: A summary of deaths within 30 days of last study treatment is provided in Table 26. In GCT1015-04, 8 subjects (7.9%) died within 30 days of last study treatment; 5 of these deaths were due to progression of underlying disease. All the deaths in Pool 1 (1 death), 6 of the 9 deaths in Pool 2, and 8 of 11 deaths in Pool 3 were due to progression of the underlying disease. Note that subjects were not followed for survival in the GEN701 and GEN702 studies.
L1018: Narrative summaries for each TEAE leading to death are provided in the respective CSRs.
L1019: In GCT1015-04, 2 subjects died due to non-TEAEs (cardiac arrest and malignant neoplasm progression) occurring &gt;30 days after last dose and thus were not reflected in the ISS (GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.03.14; Listing 16.02.03.04). The event of cardiac arrest was considered treatment-related by the investigator. This case concerns a 61-year-old subject with cervical cancer and metastases to the abdomen (carcinosis). Medical history included heavy smoking, asthmatic bronchitis (treated with salbutamol inhalation), and low BMI (approximately 15). Fourteen days after first dose of tisotumab vedotin, the subject started experiencing shortness of breath. A CT scan of the thorax performed at Day 21 showed several pulmonary embolisms bilaterally and treatment with anticoagulation was initiated. The subject was discharged the same day and continued anticoagulation therapy at home. The subject was followed-up as an outpatient. Planned trial imaging performed on Day 36 showed worsening of peritoneal disease, worsening ascites, and clots in both lungs. A paracentesis was planned to occur on Day 42; however, on Day 41 the subject died at home. An autopsy showed cervical cancer with direct spread to soft tissue in the small pelvis and peritoneum to be the underlying cause of death.

L1023: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1024: TEAEs leading to death were infrequent. Most TEAEs leading to death were considered not related to tisotumab vedotin therapy. The single case of a drug-related AE leading to death was confounded by comorbidities and underlying disease.
L1025: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1026: FDA performed an independent analysis of all patient deaths due to TEAEs and selected other patient deaths that occurred in GCT1015-04. In GCT1015-04, there were 58 patients who died, including 8 deaths on study treatment and 50 deaths post-treatment. Of the 8 deaths which occurred on study treatment, 4 were due to progression of disease, and 4 were due to an adverse event. Of the 50 deaths which occurred post-treatment, 48 were due to progression of disease, 1 was due to euthanasia due to progression of disease, and 1 was due to an adverse event. FDA reviewed narratives for all 4 deaths due to adverse events which occurred on study treatment. FDA attributes these 4 deaths from adverse events to septic shock related to tisotumab vedotin, pneumonitis potentially related to tisotumab vedotin, sudden death of uncertain cause, and multisystem organ failure related to ileus. These 4 deaths are included in Section 6.1 of product labeling. The narratives describing the deaths are summarized below:
L1027: ## **Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b)(6) Septic shock**
L1028: This 64 yo woman had metastatic SCC of the cervix involving liver, lung, and pelvic and extra-pelvic lymph nodes at study entry. She received the first cycle of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 10, she was admitted to the ICU with neutropenic sepsis and received antibiotics and antifungal treatment. CT showed signs of colitis and she underwent colonoscopy on Day 12 and Day 16. Pathologic samples from each colonoscopy confirmed diagnosis of colitis. On study day 17, patient developed septic shock, and on day 20 she died. FDA agrees with the investigator and Sponsor that cause of death was septic shock related to tisotumab vedotin.
L1029: ## **Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b)(6) Sudden death**
L1030: This 59 yo woman with history of atrial fibrillation and hypothyroidism had metastatic SCC of the cervix involving the cervix, peritoneum, and extra-pelvic lymph nodes at study entry. She
L1031: received three cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 43, CT showed stable disease. On study day 47, she died at home after reporting “feeling weak” for the past 2 or 3 days. There was no autopsy performed and cause of death was reported as unknown. The investigator and Sponsor state that death was not related to tisotumab vedotin. FDA considers it impossible to make a determination and cannot make a more specific determination regarding cause of death beyond sudden death.
L1032: ## **Patient ID: GCT1015-04- (b) (6) Pneumonitis**
L1033: This 70 yo woman with history of type 2 diabetes mellitus had metastatic SCC of the cervix involving lungs, bone, rectum, and pelvis. She received two cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 27, she was admitted to the hospital with fever and shortness of breath and was diagnosed with pneumonitis based on chest CT. CT also showed lung metastases and a L-sided pleural effusion, size unspecified. She received treatments with antibiotics, prednisone, and furosemide. The narrative states that the patient was believed to be at the end-of-life and was discharged from the hospital. It is not clear if she was discharged to hospice, but on day 42 she received palliative sedation with morphine and on day 45 she died. FDA disagrees with the investigator and Sponsor and thinks that pneumonitis and death are possibly related to tisotumab vedotin.
L1034: ## **Patient ID: GCT1015-04- (b) (6) Multiorgan failure and ileus**
L1035: This 59 yo woman had metastatic SCC of the cervix involving the cervix, kidney, and extra-pelvic lymph nodes. She received thirteen cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 271, she experienced nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, and diarrhea, and was diagnosed with ileus. She was treated with a partial intestinal resection and during surgery, she developed multiorgan failure and circulatory instability, and died. The investigator attributed her death to deterioration of underlying cancer disease, ileus, multiorgan failure, and circulatory instability. FDA agrees with this description and attributes this patient's death to multiorgan failure.

L1042: In GCT1015-04, 13 subjects (12.9%) experienced at least 1 treatment-related SAE (ISS Table 10.2.11). The rate of treatment related SAEs was similar between GCT1015-04 and all cervical cancer subjects in Pool 2, but was lower in GCT1015-04 (12.9%) than in the other pooled analysis groups (22.8% in Pool 1 and 20.2% in Pool 3).
L1043: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1044: Overall, the incidence of SAEs in GCT1015-04 was similar to the pooled analysis groups; nearly half of all tisotumab vedotin treated subjects (41.5%) experienced at least 1 SAE. The incidence of serious pneumonia was less frequent in the pooled analyses compared to GCT1015-04 but otherwise the rates of SAEs were similar across all groups. Across all safety analysis groups, between 12.9% and 22.8% of subjects experienced treatment related SAEs.
L1045: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1046: FDA conducted an independent analysis of SAEs which occurred on GCT1015-04. SAEs occurred frequently, affecting 43% of patients. The most common SAEs (≥3%) in FDA's analysis were ileus (6%), hemorrhage (5%), pneumonia (4%), sepsis (3%), constipation (3%), pyrexia (3%), and peripheral neuropathy (3%). Slight differences in the frequencies of SAEs between FDA's analysis and the Applicant's analysis are due to differences in grouping of preferred terms.
L1047: Ileus is a grouped term containing the following preferred terms: ileus, subileus, large intestinal obstruction, intestinal obstruction, small intestinal obstruction. Sepsis is a grouped term containing the following preferred terms: septic shock, urosepsis, and neutropenic sepsis. For grouping for hemorrhage and peripheral neuropathy, refer to the table below in "Treatment
L1048: ## Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions."
L1049: FDA does not consider attribution (treatment-related versus not) in analysis of SAEs as this is a subjective assessment and determination of causality is challenging.

L1056: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1057: Fewer discontinuations occurred in GCT1015-04 than the other safety analysis pools; this may be a result of the safety-related dose modification criteria in the GCT1015-04 trial, which may have allowed subjects in GCT1015-04 to remain on treatment longer. The events and rates observed are representative of the overall safety profile. Discontinuations due to drug-related TEAEs were infrequent, with peripheral neuropathy being the most common reason.
L1058: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1059: FDA conducted an independent analysis of TEAEs leading to discontinuation in GCT1015-04. The most common TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation (≥1%) in FDA's analysis were peripheral neuropathy (5%), corneal adverse reactions (4%), conjunctival adverse reactions (1%), eyelid disorders (1%), ileus (1%), nausea (1%), rash (1%), sepsis (1%), and vomiting (1%).
L1060: Differences between frequencies in FDA's analysis compared to the Applicant's analysis are due to differences in grouping of preferred terms. Refer to the "Serious Adverse Events" and "Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions" sections for description of preferred terms included in the following grouped terms: peripheral neuropathy, corneal adverse reactions, conjunctival adverse reactions, eyelid disorders, ileus, rash, and sepsis.
L1061: FDA agrees that TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were less common in GCT1015-04 than in the other safety pools. It is unclear if this is due to protocol changes, differences in trial populations, and/or other factors.
L1062: FDA does not consider attribution (treatment-related versus not) in the analysis of TEAEs leading to discontinuation as this is a subjective assessment and determination of causality is challenging.
L1063: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1064: ## Data:
L1065: In GCT1015-04, dose reductions occurred in more than 1 subject for ocular preferred terms, which included conjunctivitis (7 subjects), keratitis (6 subjects), dry eye (3 subjects), blepharitis (2 subjects), and punctate keratitis (2 subjects) (ISS Table 10.2.8). These dose reductions were as required by the trial protocol. Dose reductions occurred in 1 subject due to peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy and in 1 subject due to peripheral sensory neuropathy (also arising from protocol-required safety measures). The AEs leading to dose reduction in Pool 3 primarily reflect the events that occurred in the GCT1015-04 trial. Three subjects (5.3%) in Pool 1 had dose reductions due to conjunctivitis.
L1066: In GCT1015-04, 47 subjects (46.5%) had dose interruptions (ISS Table 10.2.7). The most common reasons for interruption were conjunctivitis (4 subjects), anemia (3 subjects), dry eye (3 subjects), and peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy (3 subjects). In Pool 1, 9 subjects (15.8%) had a dose interruption. The incidence of dose interruption in the safety pooled analysis data sets primarily reflects events that occurred in the GCT1015-04 trial.
L1067: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1068: Dose reductions in response to AESIs were implemented as important subject safety measures in GCT1015-04. In earlier trials (GEN701, GEN702), dose reductions in response to observed AESIs were added to protocols as amendments. As such, dose reductions were not used as extensively to manage AESIs, particularly ocular events, in the populations for the pooled analyses as they were for GCT1015-04.
L1069: Dose interruptions, particularly dose delays, were also an integral part of subject safety measures in the GCT1015-04 trial. Safety-related dose modification criteria in the GCT1015-04 trial may have contributed to a higher percentage of dose interruptions (46.5%) among these subjects relative to subjects in Pool 1 (15.8%), which may have allowed them to remain on treatment longer with fewer discontinuations due to TEAEs.
L1070: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1071: FDA conducted an independent analysis of dose reductions and interruptions in GCT105-04. In this trial, 23% of patients experienced TEAEs leading to dose reductions, and 47% of patients experienced TEAEs leading to dose interruptions. The most common reasons for dose reductions (\ge 3\%) were conjunctival adverse reactions (9%), corneal adverse reactions (8%), and dry eye (3%). The most reasons for dose interruptions (\ge 3\%) were peripheral neuropathy (8%), conjunctival adverse reactions (4%), hemorrhage (4%), acute kidney injury (3%), anemia (3%), dry eye (3%), and ileus (3%). Differences between FDA's analysis and the Applicant's analysis are due to grouping of preferred terms.
L1072: FDA agrees that one contributor to differences in frequencies of dose modifications in GCT1015-04 compared to the other safety pools could be due to protocol differences relating to implementation of dose modifications in responses to adverse events, particularly ocular adverse events.

L1080: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1081: As expected, based on underlying malignancy, anemia was the most common grade 3 and higher TEAE. The pooled analyses show similar proportions of subjects with anemia and fatigue TEAEs of grade 3 and higher. The sponsor considers the AESIs of ocular events and peripheral neuropathy to be causally associated with tisotumab vedotin and therefore also significant. These are described in later sections of this document.
L1082: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1083: FDA conducted an independent analysis for Grade ≥3 TEAEs in GCT1015-04. Grade ≥3 TEAEs occurred frequently, affecting 60% of patients. The most common Grade ≥3 TEAEs (≥3%) in FDA's analysis were: anemia (9%), peripheral neuropathy (7%), fatigue (7%), hemorrhage (6%), pneumonia (4%), ileus (4%), urinary tract infection (3%), urinary tract obstruction (3%), acute kidney injury (3%), pulmonary embolism (3%), hypocalcemia (3%), neutropenia (3%), and corneal adverse reactions (3%). Differences between FDA's analysis and the Applicant's analyses are due to grouping of preferred terms. FDA notes that when preferred terms are grouped, the frequencies of certain Grade ≥3 TEAEs including peripheral neuropathy, hemorrhage, and corneal adverse reactions are higher than reported by the Applicant.

L1093: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1094: Overall incidence of TEAEs appears generally similar across groups. Rates of epistaxis and conjunctivitis were lower in the GCT1015-04 trial, and in the case of conjunctivitis may reflect the evolving management of ocular AEs as clinical development of tisotumab vedotin progressed. Generally, the events and rates observed in the pivotal trial are similar and representative of the overall safety profile.
L1095: Tisotumab vedotin has a manageable and tolerable safety profile in subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who previously received chemotherapy doublet plus bevacizumab, if eligible. Most observed AEs were manageable with either supportive or preventative care or dose modifications.
L1096: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1097: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of TEAEs, presented in Table 30. The frequency of grade \ge 3 TEAEs is slightly higher in GCT1015-04 than in the other safety pools. The reasons for this are unclear, and FDA notes that this comparison across safety pools is limited by small sample size.
L1098: FDA conducted an independent analysis of all grade TEAEs in GCT1015-04 which is shown in Table 32. The most common TEAEs included fatigue (50%), nausea (41%), peripheral neuropathy (39%), alopecia (39%), epistaxis (39%), conjunctival adverse reactions (37%), and hemorrhage (32%). These toxicities reflect both the anti-TF target and the MMAE payload. Differences between FDA's analysis and the Applicant's analysis are due to grouping of preferred terms.

L1107: The safety profile for tisotumab vedotin contains certain unique toxicities (ocular toxicity, hemorrhage) as well toxicities more familiar to an oncology population (peripheral neuropathy, nausea, alopecia, fatigue, anemia) which require attention from patients and providers. The safety profile is manageable through labeling and routine oncologic care.
L1108: ## Laboratory Findings
L1109: ## Data:
L1110: ## Serum Chemistry:
L1111: Exposure to tisotumab vedotin did not meaningfully impact clinical laboratory results for serum chemistry. Across all safety analysis groups, shifts to grade 3 or 4 laboratory chemistry values were infrequent (5% or less). For this reason, only Pool 3 is described. Shift tables for serum chemistry values for GCT1015-04 are available in (ISS Table 10.4.1) and for safety analysis Pools 1 and 2 are located in (ISS Table 10.4.2) and (ISS Table 10.4.3), respectively.
L1112: ## Complete Blood Count:
L1113: Exposure to tisotumab vedotin did not meaningfully impact clinical laboratory results for
L1114: complete blood count. In Pool 3, the number of subjects who experienced shifts in blood count to grade 3 or 4 at any post-baseline treatment trial visit was infrequent (5% or less) with the exception of low lymphocytes (8.5% of subjects experienced shift from grade 2 to grade 3). Shift tables for blood count parameters for GCT1015-04 are available in (ISS Table 10.4.9) and for safety analysis Pools 1, 2, and 3 are located in (ISS Table 10.4.10), (ISS Table 10.4.11), and (ISS Table 10.4.12), respectively.
L1115: ## **Drug-Induced Liver Abnormalities:**
L1116: In GCT1015-04, no drug-induced liver injuries (DILI; defined as AST/ALT &gt;3×ULN and total bilirubin &gt;2×ULN and as AST/ALT &gt;3×ULN, total bilirubin &gt;2×ULN, and alkaline phosphatase &lt;1.5×ULN that occurred within 30 days of the last dose) were observed (ISS Table 10.4.13). The number of abnormal liver function tests was &lt;1% across all trials (ie, in Pool 3). One subject (701126) in Pool 3 had AST and/or ALT elevations &gt;3×ULN combined with bilirubin elevations &gt;2×ULN. The subject had a high ALP level, bile duct obstruction and did not meet the criteria consistent with the event designation of DILI.
L1117: ## **Coagulation Parameters:**
L1118: In Pool 3, 2 subjects (0.7%) experienced shifts in coagulation parameters (aPTT) to grade 3 at any post-baseline treatment trial visit in Pool 3 (ISS Table 10.4.8). Shift tables for coagulation parameters for GCT1015-04 are available in (ISS Table 10.4.5) and for safety analysis Pools 1 and 2 are located in (ISS Table 10.4.6) and (ISS Table 10.4.7), respectively. Most tisotumab vedotin trials excluded subjects with a history of bleeding events or coagulation abnormalities.
L1119: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1120: Exposure to tisotumab vedotin did not meaningfully impact clinical laboratory results for complete blood count or serum chemistry. Most tisotumab vedotin trials excluded subjects with a history of coagulation defects that increased the risk of bleeding as well as subjects with ongoing major bleeding (ie, symptomatic bleeding in critical area organ or bleeding causing a drop in hemoglobin level of ≥2 g/dL or that required transfusion of ≥2 units).
L1121: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1122: FDA's laboratory analysis was based on GCT1015-04 results only and FDA conducted an independent analysis of laboratory abnormalities on this trial which is shown in Table 33. Most laboratory abnormalities were low-grade, and grade ≥3 laboratory abnormalities were infrequent. The most common Grade≥3 laboratory abnormalities were lymphocytes decreased (8%), anemia (7%), creatinine increased (4%), creatinine kinase increased (2%), magnesium decreased (2%), and aPTT prolonged (2%).
