L1128: ## Vital Signs
L1129: ## Data:
L1130: Vital sign measurements, including heart rate, diastolic and systolic blood pressure, and temperature, are summarized or listed individually in each CSR for the trials included in the safety analysis groups. Any clinically significant changes were captured as AEs.
L1131: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1132: Tisotumab vedotin did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on vital signs during any clinical trial.
L1133: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1134: FDA did not conduct an independent analysis of vital sign data and believes that any clinically significant changes would be captured as TEAEs.
L1135: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1136: ## Data:
L1137: High-quality triplicate ECG measures were collected in studies GCT1015-04 and SGNTV-001 at a dose of 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W. In GCT1015-04, no meaningful changes in ECG interpretation (normal vs abnormal) were observed over time. In the SGNTV-001 trial, none of the subjects had any ECG change from baseline of more than 60 ms.
L1138: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1139: No clinically significant changes in ECG readings were detected after treatment with tisotumab vedotin.
L1140: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1141: Tisotumab vedotin treatment at 2 mg/kg Q3W does not cause large mean increases (&gt;20 msec) in the QTc interval. Please refer to the FDA QT IRT by Nan Zheng and Christine Garnett dated 4/21/21 review for further information.
L1142: ## QT
L1143: ## Data:
L1144: The effect of tisotumab vedotin on the duration of cardiac ventricular repolarization was evaluated in GCT1015-04 and SGNTV-001. This analysis was conducted on PK and ECG data from 153 subjects with solid tumors. The relationship between ADC and MMAE concentrations and change in QTcF intervals from baseline (\DeltaQTcF) was explored using linear mixed effects modeling. The concentration-\DeltaQTcF analysis did not show a clinically relevant \DeltaQTcF risk at Cmax of both ADC and MMAE following the administration of tisotumab vedotin at a dose of 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, as the upper bounds of one-sided 95% CI were below 10 msec for all analytes.
L1145: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1146: Tisotumab vedotin, administered at the recommended dose of the 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, does not have a clinically meaningful effect on the QTc prolongation.
L1147: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1148: Based on the FDA QT IRT's analysis, tisotumab vedotin treatment at 2 mg/kg Q3W IV does not cause large mean increases (&gt;20 msec) in the QTc interval. Please refer to the FDA QT IRT review dated 4/21/21 for further information.
L1149: ## **Immunogenicity**
L1150: ## **Data:**
L1151: Serum samples of subjects from the GCT1015-04, as well as ADA Pool 1 and ADA Pool 2, were analyzed for ADAs to tisotumab vedotin during the course of clinical development. ADA Pool 2 also included data from 56 subjects in the SGNTV-001 trial to enlarge the dataset to 305 subjects and include subjects that were a part of the PopPK analysis.
L1152: In GCT1015-04, approximately 2% of subjects were positive for ADAs at baseline. Across all ADA Pools, the percentage of subjects positive for ADAs at baseline ranged from 2.1% to 3.9% (ISS Table 10.4.14). In GCT1015-04, approximately 5% of the subjects were confirmed to have treatment emergent ADAs. 4.3% of subjects were confirmed to be positive for treatment-emergent ADA in the ADA Pool 2.
L1153: A total of 305 subjects were tested for ADA; 13 subjects (4.3%) were confirmed to be positive for ADA at any post-baseline timepoint (ISS Table 10.4.14).
L1154: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1155: Overall, the rate of immunogenicity to tisotumab vedotin was low. The presence of ADA had no clinically meaningful effect on tisotumab vedotin pharmacokinetics. There is no evidence of a significant safety risk associated with immunogenicity.
L1156: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1157: In Study GCT1015-04, 5 of 93 (5.4%) patients who had immunogenicity assessment at baseline and at least one time point post-dose, developed treatment-emergent ADA against tisotumab vedotin. Two of these 5 patients in Study 04, tested positive for neutralizing antibodies against tisotumab vedotin. Across studies, 145 patients with cervical cancer treated at the proposed 2 mg/kg Q3W IV dosage in Studies GEN701, GEN702, 04 had immunogenicity assessment at baseline and at least one time point post-dose. Eight of 145 patients (5.5%) developed treatment-emergent ADA against tisotumab vedotin. Due the limited number of patients who were positive for ADA during treatment with tisotumab vedotin, the impact of ADA on the PK, safety and efficacy of tisotumab vedotin is not fully characterized.
L1158: ## 8.2.4 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1159: ## 8.2.4.1 Ocular Events/Toxicity
L1160: ## **Data:**
L1161: Ocular AEs are a known safety risk of tisotumab vedotin treatment. Analyses of ocular AEs were
L1162: conducted across safety analysis populations using the following MedDRA specifications.
L1163: - Conjunctival disorders SMQ (Narrow) [20000175]
- Corneal disorders SMQ (Broad) [20000156]
- Glaucoma SMQ (Broad) [20000146]
- Lacrimal disorders SMQ (Narrow) [20000176]
- Ocular infections SMQ (Broad) [20000183]
- Optic nerve disorders SMQ (Broad) [20000148]
- Periorbital and eyelid disorders SMQ (Narrow) [20000179]
- Retinal disorders SMQ (Broad) [20000158]
- Scleral disorders SMQ (Narrow) [20000182]
- Eye Disorders SOC [10015919]
L1164: In GCT1015-04, 55 subjects (54.5%) had at least one ocular event (Table 33). Treatment-related ocular events were reported in all but one of these subjects. Most ocular AEs were confined to the ocular surface (ie, conjunctival, corneal, periorbital, and eyelid disorders). The most common ocular TEAEs (\ge5% incidence) for subjects in the GCT1015-04 trial were conjunctivitis (30.7%), dry eye (24.8%), keratitis (10.9%), blepharitis (6.9%), and punctate keratitis (5.9%) (ISS Table 10.2.38).
L1165: In GCT1015-04, most ocular events were grade 1 (22.8%) or grade 2 (28.7%) in severity, were transient, and achieved resolution. Three subjects (3.0%) had grade 3 ocular events; all of which were ulcerative keratitis (ISS Table 10.2.42). There were no grade 4 ocular events. No subjects experienced ocular SAEs (ISS Table 10.2.39). Five subjects (5.0%) discontinued tisotumab vedotin due to ocular AEs (2 subjects due to ulcerative keratitis, 1 subject due to corneal scar, 1 subject due to conjunctivitis, and 1 subject due to keratitis, entropion, and trichiasis) (ISS Table 10.2.45).
L1166: In GCT1015-04, 72.7% of subjects with ocular events experienced complete resolution of all ocular events. All remaining subjects had improvement or resolution of at least 1 ocular event. Median time to resolution was 0.59 months (range 0.0 to 11.1 months). At the last follow-up, of the 55 subjects who experienced ocular AEs, 12 (21.8%) had ongoing ocular AEs with residual maximum grade 1, 2 (3.6%) had grade 2, and 1 subject (1.8%) had grade 3.
L1167: Overall, the incidence of ocular events was numerically lower in GCT1015-04 compared to the pooled analysis groups, with the highest rate observed in Pool 1 (70.2%). Grade 3 ocular events were similar, occurring in 1.8% to 3.3% of subjects across all safety analysis groups. Time to onset was longer, time to resolution was shorter, and a greater proportion of events resolved in GCT1015-04 compared to the pooled analysis groups (Table 33). Rates of treatment discontinuation due to ocular AEs were generally similar between pivotal trial GCT1015-04 and the pooled safety analysis groups (range 5.0% to 8.8% across all groups).
L1168: In Pool 3, symblepharon was noted in 3 subjects (1.1%) with other tumor types treated with tisotumab vedotin 2.0 mg/kg, and 2 subjects (0.7%) experienced serious conjunctivitis.

L1171: Source: Adapted from ISS Table 10.2.48; Table 10.2.42; Table 10.2.43
L1172: In GCT1015-04, approximately half of subjects (47 subjects, 46.5%) had pre-existing ocular AEs at the baseline visit as assessed by an ophthalmologist. Subjects with pre-existing ocular AEs did not report an increase in ocular TEAEs while on study compared to the overall study population (Table 34) and the incidence was similar to subjects without a preexisting event.
L1173: Best corrected visual acuity (BCVA) was measured in the GCT1015-04 trial at baseline and as appropriate at the time of the ocular AE. A total of 55 subjects developed ocular TEAEs, of which 49 had both baseline and post-baseline visual acuity exam. In total, 3 subjects (3.0%) experienced visual acuity (VA) Snellen score(s) \ge 20/50 post-baseline. Two subjects experienced unilateral VA \ge 20/50 \le 20/200 post-baseline. One subject experienced bilateral VA \ge 20/50 postbaseline, with unilateral VA of 20/200 (improved to 20/40 after 7 days). BCVA remained normal for the majority of subjects who had ocular AEs (see [GCT1015-04 CSR Table 14.03.29]).
L1174: ## *Impact of Protocol-Defined OMP*
L1175: Protocol-defined ocular mitigation plan (OMP) measures intended to limit the risk of ocular AEs with tisotumab vedotin administration have evolved over the course of the clinical development program. The initial version of a protocol-defined OMP for tisotumab vedotin was introduced in the GEN701 and GEN702 trials in June 2016, with additional measures added in October 2016, December 2016, July 2017, and March 2018 (see July 2019 FDA Information Amendment, submitted to IND (b) (6) Sequence # (b) (4)). Thus, ocular safety data from trials that enrolled subjects before or during the time period of OMP refinement may reflect ongoing changes. The integrated safety pooled analysis groups include subjects from GEN701 and GEN702 who were treated during this period of OMP refinement. The GCT1015-04 trial began enrollment in June 2018. The current and most comprehensive protocol-defined OMP in GCT1015-04 was implemented from initiation onward and all subjects treated in GCT1015-04 received this current OMP. It included prophylactic measures (vasoconstrictor eye drops and cold packs during infusion as well as steroid and lubricating eye drops throughout each treatment cycle), symptom-induced ophthalmological exams, and dose modifications based on low grade ocular events.
L1176: Ocular TEAEs in subjects by OMP status at Cycle 1 (per the defined measures in the GCT1015-04 protocol) are summarized in Table 35. Overall, results showed that when the current and most comprehensive OMP was implemented as per the GCT1015-04 protocol, subjects had a reduced and consistent incidence of ocular TEAEs (range 54.5% to 58.5% across groups) compared to subjects who received earlier or variable implementation of the OMP (75.0% in Pool 2 and 72.6% in Pool 3). The reduction in ocular TEAEs seen with the current OMP appeared primarily driven by the difference in reports of conjunctivitis (reported in 30.3% of subjects with GCT1015-04 protocol-defined OMP at Cycle 1 versus 53.6% of subjects without in Pool 3) (ISS Table 10.3.12). Corneal disorders were consistent across all groups and irrespective of the OMP (range 18.8% to 21.1%) with the exception of Pool 3 wherein they were reported in 3.6% of subjects who had not received the current OMP measures at Cycle 1. Preferred terms under corneal disorders included keratitis, punctate keratitis, ulcerative keratitis, and vital dye staining cornea present. In addition, a small numerical increase in dry eye was observed (range 24.5% to 26.8%) in subjects who received the OMP as per the GCT1015-04 protocol versus those subjects who did not (12.5% in Pool 2 and 21.4% in Pool 3) (ISS Table 10.3.12).
L1177: Most ocular events were grade 1 or 2 regardless of OMP status at Cycle 1 (Table 35). Subjects who received the protocol-defined OMP at Cycle 1 had a reduced frequency of grade 2 and higher ocular events (range 31.7% to 36.7%) versus subjects who did not (50.0% in Pool 2 and 48.8% in Pool 3).
L1178: The rate of treatment discontinuations due to ocular events in subjects receiving tisotumab vedotin with the current OMP was approximately half of that in subjects who received initial or earlier OMP measures (5.3% versus 9.5% in Pool 3) (Table 34). Dose reductions were more frequent in subjects who received the current OMP (range 14.4% to 19.8%) than those who did not (6.3% in Pool 2 and 6.0% in Pool 3). Dose modifications and proactive ophthalmology
L1179: consultation in response to ocular events are part of the protocol-defined OMP and may have enabled a longer duration of therapy for tisotumab vedotin.
L1180: Time to onset for any grade 2 and higher ocular AEs was longer for subjects who received the OMP in Cycle 1 in Pool 3 (ISS Table 10.3.14). Median time to onset of the first ocular event leading to treatment discontinuation in Pool 3 was longer in subjects who received the current OMP (4.42 months) compared to subjects who did not (0.90 months) (ISS Table 10.3.14).
L1181: Exposure-adjusted toxicity analysis of Pool 3 showed lower incidence of ocular TEAEs in subjects who received the OMP vs those who did not (495 vs 564 ocular AEs per 100 patient-years) (ISS Table 10.3.16). Grade 3 and higher ocular TEAEs showed a similar trend (8 vs 17 AEs per 100 patient-years with OMP vs without OMP). Additionally, the rate of discontinuations was lower for subjects who received the OMP as compared to those who did not (23 vs 39 per 100 patient-years). Subjects across all trials (ie, Pool 3) who received the current OMP at Cycle 1 as per GCT1015-04 had lower rates of conjunctivitis (132 events per 100 person-years vs 241 events) and dry eye (77 events per 100 person-years vs 82 events) (ISS Table 10.3.15).

L1185: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1186: Most ocular events were grade 1 or 2, were transient, and achieved resolution. Three subjects (3.0%) had grade 3 ocular events in GCT1015-04 and this rate was similar across the pooled safety groups (1.8% to 3.3%). There were no grade 4 ocular events in any study with 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W dosing and use of the protocol-defined OMP. Rates of conjunctivitis were lower in the
L1187: GCT1015-04 trial, which may result from evolving management of ocular AEs as clinical development of tisotumab vedotin progressed.
L1188: In the GCT1015-04 study, all subjects received the current, comprehensive OMP. This OMP includes preventive eye drops, cooling eye packs, prompt assessment by eye care specialists upon occurrence of ocular symptoms, and dose modifications. Time-to-onset and exposure adjusted analyses suggest that, with use of the comprehensive OMP, onset of ocular TEAEs was delayed, these events were less frequent, and fewer treatment discontinuations occurred.
L1189: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1190: FDA disagrees with portions of the Applicant's statements. FDA conducted an independent analysis of ocular toxicity focusing on the GCT1015-04 and Pool 2 safety populations. FDA's search strategy for ocular toxicity relied on identifying preferred terms which connotated ocular toxicity and this strategy produced results similar to the Applicant's for overall frequency of ocular toxicity in GCT1015-04 and in Pool 2, including the frequencies of Grade 1 and Grade 2 adverse reactions.
L1191: The most common ocular toxicities in Pool 2 were: conjunctival adverse reactions (40%), dry eye (28%), and corneal adverse reactions (21%). The frequencies of these events were similar in the GCT1015-04 population. Differences in frequencies reported by the FDA and the Applicant were due to grouping of preferred terms. FDA's groupings of preferred terms are listed at the end of this section. Ocular toxicity led to discontinuation of tisotumab vedotin in 6% of patients in the Pool 2 population.
L1192: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's description of Grade 3 and Grade 4 ocular toxicity in the GCT1015-04 and Pool 2 safety populations. With respect to Grade 3 ocular toxicity, in addition to the n=3 patients with grade 3 ulcerative keratitis (b)(6) in GCT1015-04 and n=1 additional patient with grade 3 conjunctivitis in Pool 2 reported by the Applicant, there was n=1 patient who had grade 3 ulcerative keratitis (b)(6) in GCT1015-04 in the 90-day DSUR. There were also n=2 patients who developed corneal ulcers (grade 3 keratitis) &gt;30 days after the last dose of tisotumab vedotin (b)(6) in GCT1015-04. Although these toxicities fall outside the treatment-emergent window, FDA considered these events in the overall safety assessment of tisotumab vedotin.
L1193: Additionally, in the 90-day safety update, a patient enrolled to GCT1015-04 who had unresolved grade 3 keratitis (b)(6) in her L eye with diminished vision to 20/80 experienced worsening to grade 4 keratitis. She experienced rupturing of her corneal ulcer requiring corneal transplantation.
L1194: In summary, in the FDA's analysis, there were an overall n=5 patients (5%) with a worst-grade grade 3 toxicity and n=1 (1%) patient with a worst-grade grade 4 toxicity in GCT1015-04, and n=1 additional patient with grade 3 toxicity in the Pool 2 population. The overall frequency of grade 3 toxicity and grade 4 toxicity in GCT1015-04 is 5% and 1%, and in Pool 2 is 3.8% and
L1195: 0.6%. These findings underscore that tisotumab vedotin may be associated with severe ocular toxicity and the toxicity may persist and worsen even after treatment is withdrawn.
L1196: FDA disagrees with the Applicant that only 3 patients experienced visual acuity changes \ge 20/50 post-baseline. FDA sent the Applicant an information request on May 11, 2021 regarding changes in visual acuity for patients enrolled to GCT1015-04. FDA identified five patients with visual acuity changes to \ge 20/50 post-baseline, summarized in the table below. There were two patients with ongoing changes at last assessment. Patient (b)(6)'s ocular toxicity is described above. Patient (b)(6) had ongoing visual acuity changes and G2 conjunctivitis and had also been diagnosed with G1 cataracts while on trial. She also developed bilateral corneal ulcers which were not reported as a TEAE as they were discovered &gt;30 days after the final dose of tisotumab vedotin.

L1198: FDA agrees with the Applicant that 55% of patients experiencing ocular toxicity in Pool 2 had resolution to baseline, and this frequency was even higher in GCT1015-04 with 73% of patients having resolution to baseline. Among the n=6 patients who developed Grade 3 ocular toxicity in Pool 2, n=4 patients experienced resolution. Data on resolution are not available for the n=2 patients with grade 3 keratitis detected in the non-treatment-emergent period in GCT105-04. The patient with Grade 4 toxicity initially had grade 3 ulcerative keratitis which worsened and required corneal transplantation. This toxicity was only considered resolved following this procedure.
L1199: FDA reviewed the protocol-defined OMP measures. FDA agrees that the frequency of ocular toxicity was lower, and more patients experienced resolution of toxicity in GCT1015-04 compared to Pool 2 and Pool 3, likely due to changes in the OMP measures. However, as noted by the FDA Ophthalmology consultant, the OMP measures also carry risk. The review team requested input from Ophthalmology and the consultant had the following (paraphrased)
L1200: ## comments:
L1201: *OMP measures resulted in decrease of ocular TEAEs. However, the OMP measures carry risks. Ophthalmic corticosteroids can increase the development of cataracts and increases the risk of sight-threatening keratitis and corneal ulcers. [The oncology review team notes that 2 patients enrolled to GCT1015-04 developed cataracts and one patient developed herpes keratitis.] Ophthalmic corticosteroids should be used consistent with current labeling which includes a slit lamp exam prior to the initial prescription and prior to all renewals.*
L1202: FDA assessed that the time to onset and time to resolution was similar across the safety populations based on the Applicant's analysis, and the impact of OMP measures on these parameters is unclear. FDA did not conduct independent exposure-adjusted toxicity analyses.
L1203: In summary, ocular toxicity occurred frequently in patients receiving tisotumab vedotin. Although most toxicities were grade 1 or 2, FDA notes that a grade 2 event indicates a moderate decrease in visual acuity and can have a significant impact on quality-of-life. Patients enrolled to GCT1015-04 received the most up-to-date OMP measures and even in this trial population, five patients experienced a grade 3 toxicity (keratitis), and one patient experienced a grade 4 toxicity (keratitis). Additionally, five patients experienced visual acuity changes \ge 20/50. After stopping tisotumab vedotin, the patient with grade 4 keratitis required corneal transplantation, and one patient continued to experience bilateral visual acuity changes \ge 20/50.
L1204: To alert providers to the potential of severe ocular toxicity in patients receiving tisotumab vedotin, ocular toxicity is included as a Boxed Warning in the USPI. FDA recommends adherence to the premedication and required eye care plan, prompt referral to an eye care professional for any new or worsening symptoms, and dose modifications if indicated.
L1205: ## *Preferred terms*
L1206: FDA grouped the following preferred terms as conjunctival adverse reactions: conjunctivitis, conjunctival abrasion, conjunctival erosion, conjunctival hyperemia, conjunctival scar, noninfective conjunctivitis, ocular hyperemia, and conjunctival hyperemia. FDA grouped the following preferred terms for dry eye: dry eye and lacrimation increased. FDA grouped the following preferred terms for corneal adverse reactions: keratitis, punctate keratitis, ulcerative keratitis, corneal erosion, corneal scar, keratopathy, corneal bleeding, cornea irritation, corneal thinning, and vital dye staining cornea present.
L1207: ## 8.2.4.2 Peripheral Neuropathy
L1208: ## Data:
L1209: Peripheral neuropathy is a known safety risk of tisotumab vedotin treatment. The cytotoxic component of the tisotumab vedotin ADC, MMAE, is a microtubule-disrupting agent. Peripheral
L1210: neuropathy is associated with microtubule-disrupting ADCs, including tisotumab vedotin. The rate of treatment-emergent peripheral neuropathy reported varies depending on the MMAE ADC. Peripheral neuropathy is also associated with other anti-cancer treatments like taxanes and platinum compounds including cisplatin and carboplatin, which are commonly used in cervical cancer treatment.
L1211: In the GCT1015-04 trial, all subjects had been treated previously with systemic therapy. Prior anticancer therapy included bevacizumab (69.3%), cisplatin (86.1%), carboplatin (65.3%) and paclitaxel (100.0%) (m5.3.5.2 GCT1015-04 Table 14.01.07.02). The majority of subjects (81.2%) had received previous radiation therapy (ISS Table 10.1.3). Peripheral neuropathy events were therefore solicited in the pivotal GCT1015-04 trial and denoted as a secondary endpoint in the GEN701 and GEN702 trials, as specified in the protocols.
L1212: The safety analysis of peripheral neuropathy events in subjects treated with 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W focused on the broad SMQ of peripheral neuropathy (20000034).
L1213: Peripheral neuropathy TEAEs are summarized in Table 36. The most common (\ge 4.0\% of subjects) peripheral neuropathy preferred terms in GCT1015-04 were neuropathy peripheral (13 subjects, 12.9%), peripheral sensory neuropathy (11 subjects, 10.9%), peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy (6 subjects, 5.9%), and paraesthesia (4 subjects, 4.0%) (ISS Table 10.2.49.1).
L1214: In GCT1015-04, peripheral neuropathy AESIs were mostly grade 1 and 2 (18.8% and 11.9%, respectively), and were manageable with dose modifications and standard supportive care. Grade 3 peripheral neuropathy events were experienced by 7 subjects (6.9%) in GCT1015-04; all were considered to be treatment related. No subjects experienced grade 4 or 5 events of peripheral neuropathy. SAEs of peripheral neuropathy were reported in 3 subjects (3.0%) (peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy in 2 subjects and peripheral motor neuropathy in 1 subject (ISS Table 10.2.50.1). Narratives for SAEs can be found in the individual CSRs.
L1215: As per the GCT1015-04 protocol, events of initial or worsening of pre-existing condition to grade 2 and 3 peripheral neuropathy were to lead to dose interruption until the event had improved to grade 1 or lower. Events of grade 4 and higher peripheral neuropathy were to lead to the permanent discontinuation of tisotumab vedotin (m5.3.5.2 GCT1015-04 Protocol Section 7.2.2). Five subjects (5.0%) discontinued, none due to a grade 4 event; the most common preferred terms leading to discontinuation were peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy and peripheral sensory neuropathy (each in 2 subjects, 2.0%) (ISS Table 10.2.56.1). Eight subjects (7.9%) experienced events leading to a dose interruption and 2 subjects (2.0%) had peripheral neuropathy TEAEs that led to a dose reduction (ISS Table 10.2.57.1 and ISS Table 10.2.58.1).
L1216: In GCT1015-04, approximately half (16 subjects) experienced improvement or resolution of at least one peripheral neuropathy event within the 30-day follow-up period after end of treatment (18.4% all resolved, 23.7% some resolved or improved). Median time to resolution was 0.49 months (range 0 to 4.8 months) (Table 36). At the last follow-up, 18/38 subjects (47.4%) had ongoing events with residual maximum grade 1, 8 (21.1%) had grade 2, and 5 (13.2%) had grade 3. It should be noted that TEAEs of peripheral neuropathy were followed
L1217: only until the safety follow-up visit (30 days after last dose), which may explain the relatively high number of events that had not yet resolved at time of last follow-up.
L1218: The incidence of peripheral neuropathy events was generally similar between GCT1015-04 and Pools 2 and 3 (any grade ranged 37.6% to 43.0%, grade 3 ranged 6.9% to 10.5%). The median time to onset of grade 2 and 3 peripheral neuropathy events was longer in GCT1015-04 (4.57 months) compared to the pooled analysis groups (median range 2.68 to 3.83 months) (ISS Table 10.3.19).

L1222: In GCT1015-04, 20 out of 101 subjects had pre-existing peripheral neuropathy TEAEs (ISS Table 10.3.17). Subjects with pre-existing peripheral neuropathy had a similar rate of
L1223: treatment-emergent peripheral neuropathy compared to the overall study population (Table 25) as well as those without pre-existing peripheral neuropathy.
L1224: These results from the pivotal trial were generally consistent with Pool 3 (ISS Table 10.3.17).
L1225: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1226: Peripheral neuropathy AESIs were mostly grade 1 and 2 and were manageable with dose modifications and standard supportive care. Across pools, the most common TEAE leading to treatment discontinuation was peripheral neuropathy. Subjects with pre-existing peripheral neuropathy had a similar rate and maximum severity of treatment-emergent peripheral neuropathy as subjects without prior history.
L1227: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1228: FDA conducted an independent analysis of peripheral neuropathy associated with tisotumab vedotin. FDA's analysis focused on the GCT1015-04 population and the Pool 2 population. Any instance of new or worsening peripheral neuropathy was captured as a TEAE. In the Pool 2 population, the frequency of peripheral neuropathy was 42%, with 8% of patients experiencing grade 3 events. The frequencies of all grade and grade 3 events were similar in the GCT1015-04 population. The most common peripheral neuropathy preferred terms in the Pool 2 population were neuropathy peripheral (20%), peripheral sensory neuropathy (11%), and peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy (5%).
L1229: In Pool 2, peripheral neuropathy led to dose interruption in 6% of patients, dose reduction in 1% of patients, and dose discontinuation in 8% of patients. Only 17% of patients who experienced peripheral neuropathy had complete resolution at last follow-up, and no patients who experienced grade 3 neuropathy had complete resolution. FDA does not consider time to resolution to be helpful or accurate information, given the large number of patients with ongoing peripheral neuropathy at last follow-up.
L1230: In GCT1015-04, information regarding whether patients had pre-existing peripheral neuropathy was captured. Peripheral neuropathy occurred in approximately equal frequency in patients with and without pre-existing peripheral neuropathy: 35% vs. 38%, respectively. Grade 3 neuropathy was more common in patients with pre-existing neuropathy compared to patients without pre-existing neuropathy: 15% vs. 5%, respectively. Patients with pre-existing peripheral neuropathy grade \ge 2 were not eligible to receive tisotumab vedotin.
L1231: FDA's analysis also considered whether all observed peripheral neuropathy was axonal, or whether tisotumab vedotin may also be associated with a demyelinating neuropathy. Axonal neuropathy is characterized predominantly by sensory loss, whereas demyelinating neuropathy is autoimmune in nature and may be associated with muscle weakness. To assess for a demyelinating neuropathy signal, FDA did a focused search for preferred terms that indicated a predominantly motor neuropathy or demyelinating neuropathy. In the Pool 2 population, 3% of patients developed motor neuropathy, 3% of patients developed muscular weakness, and 1% of patients developed a demyelinating peripheral neuropathy. In addition, there was one patient
L1232: with a different tumor type in the Pool 3 population who developed Guillain-Barre syndrome.
L1233: Peripheral neuropathy is included in Section 5: Warnings and Precautions of the tisotumab vedotin USPI. FDA recommends discontinuing tisotumab vedotin for grade 3 or 4 peripheral neuropathy.
L1234: ## 8.2.4.3 Bleeding Events
L1235: ## **Data:**
L1236: Tisotumab vedotin is an ADC-targeting tissue factor, which is the molecular initiator of the coagulation cascade after vascular injury in normal physiology. For this reason, bleeding was identified as an AESI for tisotumab vedotin. Pelvic bleeding is a well-known complication of cervical cancer because of local tumor growth in vaginal and pelvic regions. In addition, as irradiated areas of the body are more prone to bleeding, prior pelvic radiotherapy, may increase the risk of bleeding. Bleeding events have been commonly reported in trials of therapies for cervical cancer. Patients with a history of bleeding conditions or severe bleeding events were typically excluded from trials of tisotumab vedotin.
L1237: The safety analysis of bleeding AEs in subjects treated with 2.0 mg/kg tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W focused on SMQs detailed in the ISS.
L1238: Bleeding events across safety analysis groups are summarized in Table 37. In GCT1015-04, bleeding events occurred in 57 subjects (56.4%) and were considered to be treatment-related in 39 subjects (38.6%). The most commonly occurring (&gt;20%) bleeding event was epistaxis, reported in 39 subjects (38.6%) (ISS Table 10.2.60). More than 90% of all epistaxis events (36/39 subjects) were grade 1 and did not require intervention. Other common bleeding AEs were (\ge 5\% incidence) vaginal haemorrhage (11 subjects, 10.9%) and haematuria (9 subjects 8.9%).
L1239: Six subjects (5.9%) had grade 3 bleeding events, which were mostly attributable to underlying illness. No subjects reported bleeding events of grade 4 or 5 (ISS Table 10.2.64). Bleeding SAEs were reported in 5 subjects (5.0%) and included preferred terms of vaginal haemorrhage (2 subjects), and cystitis haemorrhagic, haematuria, and rectal haemorrhage (each in 1 subject) (ISS Table 10.2.61). Narratives for SAEs can be found in the individual CSRs. No subjects in GCT1015-04 discontinued due to bleeding AEs (ISS Table 10.2.67).
L1240: In GCT1015-04, 45/57 (78.9%) experienced complete resolution of all their events and a further 6 subjects (10.5%) had improvement or resolution of at least 1 event of bleeding (Table 37). Median time to resolution was 0.33 months (range 0.0 to 6.1 months). At the last follow-up, 8/57 (14.0%) had AEs with residual maximum grade 1, 2 (3.5%) had AEs with residual maximum grade 2, and 2 (3.5%) had AEs with residual maximum grade 3.
L1241: These results from GCT1015-04 were generally consistent with Pool 2. The rate of bleeding AEs was lower in the GCT1015-04 group (56.4%) compared to subjects in Pool 1 (71.9%) and Pool 3 (70.6%). In addition, treatment-related bleeding events were slightly less frequent in the pivotal
L1242: trial compared to Pool 2 (46.2%) and lower than in the other pooled analyses (59.6% in Pool 1, and 58.1% in Pool 3). While no subjects with cervical cancer discontinued due to bleeding AEs, 2 subjects (0.7%) with other tumor types included in Pool 3 were discontinued due to bleeding events (epistaxis and haemoptysis in 1 subject and gastrointestinal hemorrhage in 1 subject) (ISS Table 10.2.67). Other differences included the median time to first onset of other bleeding events excluding epistaxis, which occurred later in subjects in GCT1015-04 compared to the pooled groups (median of 0.76 months vs 0.26, 0.46, and 0.30 months in Pools 1, 2, and 3, respectively) (ISS Table 10.3.4).

L1246: Major bleeding events were typically associated with tumor location, were manageable with standard supportive care (eg, transfusions), and did not require more aggressive measures such as use of clotting factors. There were no AEs of bleeding associated with fatal outcomes. In GCT1015-04, 6 subjects (5.9%) experienced grade 3 bleeding events that were generally related to tumor progression, including the preferred terms of vaginal haemorrhage, rectal haemorrhage, cystitis haemorrhagic, and haematuria (ISS Table 10.2.62 and ISS Table 10.2.66).
L1247: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1248: The most commonly occurring bleeding event was epistaxis, more than 90% of all epistaxis events were grade 1 and did not require intervention. Major bleeding events were typically associated with tumor location, were manageable with standard supportive care (eg, transfusions), and did not require more aggressive measures such as use of clotting factors. There were no AEs of bleeding associated with fatal outcomes. No subject discontinued due to Bleeding AEs in GCT1015-04 or Pool 2.
L1249: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1250: FDA conducted an independent analysis of hemorrhage which focused on patients with cervical cancer receiving tisotumab vedotin in GCT1015-04 and in Pool 2. Hemorrhage was common, with 62% of patients in the Pool 2 population experiencing hemorrhage. Although most hemorrhage TEAEs were grade 1, 5% of patients in Pool 2 experienced grade 3 events. In the 90-day safety update, there was one additional patient enrolled to GCT1015-04 who experienced grade 3 hematuria. The increased frequency of hemorrhage in the Pool 2 population compared to the GCT1015-04 population could be due to differences in patient characteristics, although FDA notes that eligibility criteria with respect to hemorrhage were similar for all trials with patients included in Pool 2.
L1251: In Pool 2, the most common types of hemorrhage were epistaxis (44%), hematuria (10%), and vaginal hemorrhage (10%). Hemorrhage occurred early after exposure to tisotumab vedotin, with median time to onset 0.3 months (range: 0, 6.5) in the Pool 2 population. Dose modifications due to hemorrhage were infrequent. In the Pool 2 population, 3% of patients experienced dose interruption and 0.6% of patients experienced dose reduction due to hemorrhage. There were no dose discontinuations due to hemorrhage.
L1252: FDA disagrees with portions of the Applicant's characterization of hemorrhage with tisotumab vedotin. Although grade 3 hemorrhage was infrequent, patients experiencing grade 3 hemorrhage required red blood cell transfusion, tranexamic acid, and more aggressive measures such as tumor resection or radiation treatment. While grade 3 hemorrhage occurred at sites of tumor, FDA believes that exposure to tisotumab vedotin may have increased the propensity of tumor to bleed at these locations based on its mechanism of action and the overall frequency of hemorrhage in patients receiving tisotumab vedotin. FDA acknowledges that there are limitations in making an assessment in a single arm trial without a control arm.
L1253: Given the frequency of hemorrhage and the potential for severity in patients receiving
L1254: tisotumab vedotin, FDA included hemorrhage in Section 5: Warnings and Precautions of the tisotumab vedotin USPI.
L1255: FDA reviewed narratives for Grade 3 hemorrhage occurring on GCT1015-04 which are summarized briefly below.
L1256: ## Narratives:
L1257: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6):
L1258: This 41 yo woman had metastatic SCC of the cervix involving the cervix, liver, and rectum. On study day -4, she had grade 1 rectal hemorrhage and grade 1 anemia. She received treatment with tranexamic acid. On study day 8, she developed grade 3 rectal hemorrhage and grade 3 anemia. She received a blood transfusion. On study day 20, the patient had rectal bleeding and a fever and was admitted to the hospital. She received another blood transfusion as well as antibiotic treatment and tisotumab vedotin administration was delayed. On study day 28, she received another blood transfusion and was discharged. On study day 30, she was readmitted with rectal hemorrhage grade 3 and she continued treatment with tranexamic acid and blood transfusions. On study day 36, rectal hemorrhage improved to grade 2 and she received cycle 2 of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 48, she was found to have radiographic progression of disease and tisotumab vedotin was discontinued. Her aPTT was within normal limits and platelets were 200 to 300 x109/L throughout the trial. Her INR was increased (1.5-1.8) and PT was slightly prolonged throughout the trial for unclear reasons.
L1259: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6)
L1260: This 75 yo woman had metastatic adenosquamous carcinoma of the cervix involving the uterus, extrapelvic lymph nodes, and lung. On day 1, after receiving tisotumab vedotin, she experienced grade 2 vaginal hemorrhage and was hospitalized. On day 4, hemorrhage improved to grade 1, and the patient underwent arterial transcatheter embolization. The procedure was unsuccessful. On day 13, the patient underwent another unsuccessful arterial transcatheter embolization. She received cycle 2 of tisotumab vedotin on study day 21. On study day 38, she underwent an epicystostomy. On study day 39, she had worsening vaginal hemorrhage to grade 3. She received red blood cell transfusion and tranexamic acid. On study days 53 to 65, the patient received palliative radiation for vaginal bleeding. Tisotumab vedotin had been discontinued due to clinical progression of disease. On study day 82, the patient died due to underlying disease. Her aPTT, PT, and INR were within normal limits throughout the trial and platelets were in the 200 to 400 x 109/L range.
L1261: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6)
L1262: This patient is a 53 yo woman with metastatic adenocarcinoma of the cervix involving the bladder, uterus, and vagina. On study day 23, the patient developed grade 3 hematuria. She was admitted and an indwelling catheter was placed for bladder irrigation. She received prophylactic enoxaparin on study day 23. On day 28, she developed grade 2 anemia. On study
L1263: day 29, the patient had transurethral resection of her bladder and received red blood cell transfusion for anemia. On study day 31, hematuria resolved. On study day 42, she received her third cycle of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 69, she discontinued tisotumab vedotin per her request. Her aPTT, PT, and INR were within normal limits throughout the trial. Her platelet count was 170-350 \times 10^9/L.
L1264: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6)
L1265: This patient is a 68 yo woman with metastatic SCC of the cervix involving the cervix, pelvic and extrapelvic lymph nodes, bone, right ventricle, and liver. She received three cycles of tisotumab vedotin. From study days 42 to 67, she was treated with dalteparin sodium for a cardiac thrombus (which was subsequently determined to be a cardiac metastasis). On study day 68, she developed grade 3 vaginal hemorrhage and was admitted. She received treatment with vaginal compression, tranexamic acid, and methylprednisolone. She had concurrent grade 3 anemia and thrombocytopenia and received transfusions of red blood cells and platelets. Her vaginal hemorrhage improved to grade 1 and she was discharged. On study day 70, she resumed dalteparin sodium. On study day 81, vaginal hemorrhage worsened to grade 3, she received transfusions with red blood cells and platelets and receive tranexamic acid. On study day 84, tisotumab vedotin was discontinued because of radiographic progression of disease. On study days 85-90, she received palliative radiation for hemorrhage. On study day 101, she died due to underlying disease.
L1266: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6)
L1267: This patient is a 42 yo woman with SCC of the cervix involving extra-pelvic lymph nodes. Following five cycles of tisotumab vedotin, she developed grade 1 lower GI hemorrhage. Following eight cycles of tisotumab vedotin, on study day 153, she developed grade 3 rectal hemorrhage, received a transfusion, and hemorrhage resolved. She continued to receive 13 cycles of tisotumab vedotin until treatment was discontinued due to radiographic progression of disease. It appears the patient received enoxaparin sodium from study day -14 to 106 for a DVT and may have also briefly received warfarin. Her platelets were between 170 and 250 \times 10^9/L for the duration of the trial.
L1268: Patient ID: GCT1015-04-(b) (6)
L1269: This patient is a 51 yo woman with SCC involving the bone and extrapelvic LN. On study day 3 she experienced grade 1 epistaxis which resolved on study day 105. She received seven cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 133, she developed grade 3 hemorrhagic cystitis. She was admitted and a urinary catheter was placed. She received TMP/SMX for treatment of cystitis and hematuria resolved. She developed grade 2 anemia. On study day 134, hematuria resolved and she was discharged. On study day 157, the patient underwent cystoscopy due to hematuria which was reported as normal. She received 12 cycles of tisotumab vedotin until treatment was discontinued due to radiographic progression of disease. She died on study day 496 due to underlying disease. Her aPTT, PT, and INR were within normal limits throughout the trial. Her platelet count was between 180 and 350 \times 10^9/L throughout the trial.
L1270: Patient ID: GEN701- (b) (6)
L1271: The patient is a 30 yo woman with cervical cancer. On study day 4, she experienced grade 1 epistaxis and this resolved on study day 6. Following eight cycles of tisotumab vedotin, on study day 178, the patient experienced grade 2 vaginal hemorrhage. This resolved on the same day and she was discharged. On study day 184, she developed grade 3 vaginal hemorrhage and this resolved on study day 187. On study day 190, MRI showed disease progression with increase in cervical tumor size and tisotumab vedotin was discontinued. Her aPTT, PT, and INR were reported as within normal limits on study day 184, and platelets were 224 \times 10^9/L
L1272: Patient ID: GEN701- (b) (6)
L1273: This patient is a 56 yo woman with cervical cancer. Following three cycles of tisotumab vedotin, on study day 69, she developed grade 3 hematuria. She received cycle four of treatment on study day 71. On study day 75, due to persistent symptoms of hematuria, the patient was admitted. A urinary catheter was placed, and the patient had a cystoscopy with transurethral resection of tumor in the bladder. There were no radiation changes visible. The patient received eight cycles of tisotumab vedotin, experience grade 2 hematuria on study day 157, and discontinued treatment on study day 177 due to progression of disease. Coagulation labs data and platelet count are not provided.
L1274: ## 8.2.4.4 Infusion-related Reactions
L1275: ## **Data:**
L1276: Infusion-related reactions were first programmatically identified in case report forms via a check box for such reactions and must have occurred the day of, or after, an infusion of tisotumab vedotin, followed by individual case clinical review to assess whether the event represented a potential infusion-related reaction (IRR.) Clinical review of each event identified took into consideration the temporal relationship to dose infusion, reported signs and symptoms, action taken with dosing (eg, interruption of infusion, reduced rate of infusion on resuming or subsequent infusion and/or introducing pre-medication), treatments or management used for the event, concurrent conditions, medical history, and recurrence of similar events on rechallenge, if available. In addition, exploratory analyses using MedDRA SMQs of Anaphylaxis and Hypersensitivity were performed.
L1277: Infusion-related reactions (IRRs) meeting the programmatic search strategy were identified in 1 subject (1.0%) in GCT1015-04 (ISS Table 10.3.57). This subject developed grade 2 fatigue and grade 2 pyrexia. The subject received subsequent doses of tisotumab vedotin with premedication without recurrence of IRR.
L1278: A total of 7 subjects were assessed as having events representing possible IRR (Table 10.3.57).
L1279: Only 1 subject from the pivotal GCT1015-04 trial experienced an episode of potential IRR reported as pyrexia and fatigue (both grade 2). The remaining 6 subjects were all from the GEN701 trial and included 2 subjects with events of hypersensitivity, 2 subjects with events of pyrexia, and 1 subject each of lethargy and hypotension. None of these events were life-threatening, and only 3 subjects received treatment with antihistamines, steroids, and/or acetaminophen. Three subjects received additional subsequent doses of tisotumab vedotin without recurrence of IRR; only 1 of which received premedication on subsequent doses.
L1280: The incidence and severity of IRRs was similar across safety analysis groups.
L1281: No events consistent with anaphylaxis/anaphylactoid reactions or severe, life threatening hypersensitivity were identified. There is no safety signal for anaphylaxis or systemic hypersensitivity with tisotumab vedotin to date.
L1282: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1283: Few subjects experienced infusion-related reactions and these were of low clinical impact and manageable by standard of care.
L1284: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1285: FDA conducted an independent analysis of IRRs associated with tisotumab vedotin which were infrequent. Given the low frequency of IRRs, FDA relied on the Pool 3 population for the assessment and agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The overall frequency of IRRs was low with 1 patient enrolled to GCT1015-04 who experienced a grade 2 IRR, and 6 additional patients in the Pool 3 population who experienced IRRs. Only one patient experienced a grade 3 IRR. Four of the 7 patients experiencing IRRs were able to get subsequent treatment with tisotumab vedotin, including the patient who experienced the grade 3 IRR.
L1286: ## 8.2.4.5 Pneumonitis
L1287: ## **The FDA's Assessment**
L1288: The Applicant did not include an assessment of pneumonitis in their submission. However, FDA conducted an independent analysis of pneumonitis as pneumonitis has been observed with other antibody-drug conjugates containing vedotin.
L1289: In the Pool 2 population, there were 2 patients who experienced pneumonitis, including 1 patient who died. Both patients were enrolled to GCT1015-04 and narratives are summarized below.
L1290: As antibody-drug conjugates containing vedotin are associated with pneumonitis, and there was one patient who received tisotumab vedotin, experienced pneumonitis and died, FDA included this toxicity in Section 5: Warnings and Precautions of the USPI.
L1291: ## **Narratives:**
L1292: ## **GCT1015-04- (b) (6) (copied from Section 8.2.3 "Deaths")**
L1293: This 70 yo woman with history of type 2 diabetes mellitus had metastatic SCC of the cervix
L1294: involving lungs, bone, rectum, and pelvis. She received two cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 27, she was admitted to the hospital with fever and shortness of breath and was diagnosed with pneumonitis based on chest CT. CT also showed lung metastases and a L-sided pleural effusion, size unspecified. She received treatments with antibiotics, prednisone, and furosemide. The narrative states that the patient was believed to be at the end-of-life and was discharged from the hospital. It is not clear if she was discharged to hospice, but on day 42 she received palliative sedation with morphine and on day 45 she died. FDA disagrees with the investigator and Sponsor and thinks that pneumonitis and death are possibly related to tisotumab vedotin.
L1295: ## **GCT1015-04-** (b) (6)
L1296: This patient is a 68 yo woman with metastatic SCC of the cervix involving the cervix, pelvic lymph nodes, extra-pelvic lymph nodes, bone, right ventricle, and liver. She received two cycles of tisotumab vedotin. On study day 41, she was diagnosed with grade 2 pneumonitis based on symptoms of dyspnea and fatigue, and a high-resolution CT scan showing bilateral diffuse ground glass infiltrates consistent with pneumonitis. She received treatment with prednisone, and on study day 78, pneumonitis was reported as resolved.
L1297: ## 8.2.5 Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1298: ## **Data:**
L1299: See Section 8.1.2 for the secondary endpoints relating to COA.
L1300: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1301: HRQOL was in general maintained or showed small but not clinically meaningful decreases on most domains between baseline and Cycle 4. The larger decreases in some domains/items at time of treatment discontinuation are expected as most subjects at the timepoint experienced progressive disease or discontinuation of trial drug due to ongoing AEs. Given the single-arm trial design, limited data collected, lack of published data on HRQOL in cervical cancer, the clinical meaningfulness of these declines is hard to interpret.
L1302: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1303: FDA did not consider HRQOL PRO data in our assessment of safety or tolerability. FDA agrees with the Applicant that interpretation of HRQOL is limited due to the lack of a control arm. Refer to Section 8.1.2 for more details on HRQOL data.
L1304: ## 8.2.6 Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1305: ## **Data:**
L1306: The safety populations were assessed with subgroup analyses for intrinsic and extrinsic factors.
L1307: ## Age
L1308: In GCT1015-04, the median age was 50 years old (m2.7.4 Table 6). Analyses were conducted for subjects &lt;65 years versus ≥65 years. In GCT1015-04, the incidence of SAEs and grade 3 and higher TEAEs was higher in the older subgroup. However, the number of subjects in the ≥65 years group was small (n=13) and therefore limits interpretation of the data. No differences by age subgroup were apparent for the most common TEAEs (ISS Table 10.2.20). The results of the pivotal trial were generally consistent with the pooled analysis groups (m2.7.4 Table 28).
L1309: ## Sex
L1310: In the safety analysis groups focused on subjects with cervical cancer, all subjects were female. In Pool 3, 46 subjects (16.9%) were male (ISS Table 10.1.2). Subgroup analyses by sex were not performed as the intended indication is cervical cancer.
L1311: ## Race and Ethnicity
L1312: Across all safety analysis groups, 89.5% to 95.0% of subjects were white, and 94.1% to 96.5% of subjects were classified as not of Hispanic or Latino ethnicity (ISS Table 10.1.2). The limited subgroup sizes for race and ethnicity in this study population preclude subgroup analysis.
L1313: ## Hepatic Insufficiency
L1314: Mild hepatic impairment at baseline was observed in a low number of subjects; 8 subjects in the GCT1015-04 trial and 31 subjects across all trials in Pool 3.
L1315: In GCT1015-04, the incidence of TEAEs and SAEs were similar regardless of baseline hepatic function, although subjects who had mild hepatic impairment had a higher incidence of grade 3 and higher TEAEs. However, the small number of subjects this group (n=8) limits interpretation of the data.
L1316: These results were consistent with the pooled safety analysis groups and there did not appear to be a notable increase in incidence of treatment emergent SAEs or grade 3 and higher TEAEs between the GCT1015-04 trial and the pooled analysis groups (m2.7.4 Table 29).
L1317: Based on population PK analysis, tisotumab vedotin exposures in subjects with mild hepatic impairment were similar to those with normal hepatic function. MMAE exposures in subjects with mild hepatic impairment were 37% higher than those with normal hepatic function. Subjects who had mild hepatic impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent SAEs or grade 3 and higher TEAEs, indicating the increase of MMAE exposure in mild hepatic impairment subjects is not clinically meaningful.
L1318: No dose modifications are required for subjects with mild hepatic impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been studied in subjects with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
L1319: ## Renal Insufficiency
L1320: In GCT1015-04, 40 subjects were enrolled who had mild renal impairment, and 15 subjects had moderate renal impairment. Subjects who had moderate renal impairment did not appear to have a notably increased incidence of treatment emergent SAEs (47% in subjects with moderate renal impairment and 46% in those with normal renal function). Similarly, moderate renal impairment did not appear to have notably increased incidence of grade 3 and higher
L1321: TEAEs (60% in subjects with moderate renal impairment and 61% of those with normal renal function).
L1322: Results from the pooled analysis groups were similar to GCT1015-04; no meaningful differences were observed between subjects with mild or moderate renal insufficiency at baseline compared to those with normal renal function.
L1323: Based on population PK analysis, tisotumab vedotin and MMAE exposures were similar in subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment compared to those with normal renal function.
L1324: No dose modifications are required for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. Tisotumab vedotin has not been studied in subjects with severe renal impairment. Therefore, the effect of severe renal impairment on tisotumab vedotin and free MMAE PK is unknown.
L1325: ## Prior Lines of Therapy
L1326: Per the ISS SAP, prior lines of therapy were not analyzed for the safety pooled analysis data sets. In GCT1015-04, most subjects (71 subjects, 70.3%) had received 1 prior line of therapy (m2.7.4, Table 31). Generally, subjects with 2 lines of prior therapy appeared to have a similar safety profile as those subjects with 1 prior line, with similar rates of TEAEs and grade 3 and higher TEAEs. The incidence of some TEAEs varied between the 2 groups; alopecia and fatigue were more frequently reported in the subjects with 2 lines of therapy, perhaps reflecting more advanced disease in these patients, while constipation was more frequent in subjects with 1 prior line. The incidence of SAEs was higher in subjects with 2 prior lines of therapy (15 subjects, 50.0%) compared to 1 prior line (28 subjects, 39.4%).
L1327: Overall, tisotumab vedotin is well tolerated in subjects with 1 to 2 prior lines of therapy in the metastatic setting.
L1328: ## Prior Bevacizumab Therapy
L1329: In GCT1015-04, most subjects (70 subjects, 69.3%) had received prior bevacizumab therapy (m2.7.4 Table 32). The overall number of SAEs and grade 3 and higher TEAEs were similar in subjects who had prior bevacizumab therapy as compared to those without it. Subjects who had prior bevacizumab had higher incidences of some TEAEs than subjects without; in the GCT1015-04 trial, 24 subjects (34%) with prior bevacizumab reported TEAEs of conjunctivitis compared to 7 subjects (23%) without (ISS Table 10.2.26). In addition, some grade 3 and higher TEAEs were more frequent in subjects with prior bevacizumab therapy, including anemia in 8 subjects (11.4%), fatigue in 5 subjects (7.1%), pneumonia in 4 subjects (5.7%), and urinary tract infection in 3 subjects (4.3%), compared to 0 subjects in those without prior bevacizumab (ISS Table 10.2.28).
L1330: The safety pooled analysis groups showed similar results. Tisotumab vedotin can be safely administered after bevacizumab or bevacizumab + chemotherapy doublet therapy.
L1331: ## **Prior Radiotherapy**
L1332: Most subjects (82 subjects, 81.2%) in GCT1015-04 received prior radiotherapy (m2.7.4, Table 33). The number of SAEs or grade 3 and higher TEAEs was similar in subjects who had prior radiotherapy compared to those who did not receive it.
L1333: Subjects who had prior radiotherapy had higher incidences of some TEAEs than subjects without. In the GCT1015-04 trial, 40 subjects (48.8%) with prior radiotherapy reported TEAEs of nausea compared to 1 subject (5.3%) without (ISS Table 10.2.23), and 34 subjects (41.5%) with prior radiotherapy experienced epistaxis compared to 5 subjects (26.3%) without (ISS Table 10.2.23). The results were generally similar across the safety pooled analysis groups.
L1334: Tisotumab vedotin was well tolerated in subjects with prior radiotherapy.
L1335: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1336: Overall, there were no safety-related differences observed between subgroups based on intrinsic factors including age, mild hepatic impairment or renal impairment that were considered to be clinically meaningful. Tisotumab vedotin has not been studied in subjects with moderate or severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment. Meaningful analysis of tisotumab vedotin safety by sex, race, and ethnicity could not be performed due to small subgroup sample sizes.
L1337: Overall, tisotumab vedotin is well tolerated in subjects with 1 to 2 prior lines of therapy in the metastatic setting and in subjects who had previously received bevacizumab or bevacizumab + chemotherapy doublet therapy. Tisotumab vedotin was well tolerated in subjects with prior radiotherapy.
L1338: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1339: FDA conducted an independent analysis of safety in patients ≥65 years compared to patients &lt;65 years. As no tumor responses were observed in patients ≥65 years receiving tisotumab vedotin, FDA was particularly concerned about risk-benefit in this patient population.
L1340: Patients ≥65 years compared to patients &lt;65 years experienced a higher frequency of grade 3-4 TEAEs and SAEs, 62% vs. 56% and 54% vs. 41%, respectively. Patients ≥ 65 years compared to patients &lt;65 years also experienced more frequent dose discontinuations: 23% vs. 11%, and dose interruptions: 62% vs. 44%. Dose reductions were the same in the two groups: 23% vs. 23%.
L1341: FDA also assessed exposure to tisotumab vedotin in patients ≥65 years compared to patients &lt;65 years to determine if decreased exposure could be driving lack of tumor responses. Patients ≥ 65 years had one fewer median number of cycles of treatment compared to patients &lt;65 years. Relative dose intensity was similar between the two age groups for cycles of treatment received. It is unclear if this difference in exposure could affect tumor response. FDA's summary of exposure to tisotumab vedotin in patients ≥65 years compared to patients &lt;65 years in shown in Table 38.
L1342: **Table 38: Exposure to tisotumab vedotin by age in GCT1015-04**
L1343: Patients <65 years	Patients ≥65 years
Duration of exposure in months, median (range)	4.3 (0.7, 16.0)	3.7 (1.3, 7.9)
Number of cycles, median (range)	6 (1, 21)	5 (2, 11)
Actual cumulative dose in mg/kg, median (range)	12.0 (2.0, 33)	9.4 (4.0, 19.2)
Relative dose intensity in %, median (range)	96.1 (43.8, 114.0)	94.4 (71.2, 105.9)
L1344: Although patients ≥ 65 years experienced higher grade TEAEs and SAEs slightly more frequently than patients &lt;65 years, this analysis is limited by the small number of patients ≥65 years enrolled to GCT1015-04. The details of the modest difference in AE incidence according to age are described in Section 8.5 of product labeling.
L1345: FDA did not analyze safety by sex as all patients with cervical cancer enrolled included in GCT1015-04 and Pool 2 were female. FDA did not analyze safety by race or ethnicity due to the small numbers of patients representing racial or ethnic minority groups enrolled to receive tisotumab vedotin.
L1346: FDA did not conduct independent analyses of safety by number of prior lines of therapy, prior bevacizumab therapy, or prior radiotherapy but did review the Applicant's analyses. In general, these subgroups are small, and the clinical relevance of differences in safety in these subgroups is uncertain. FDA does not consider the differences in safety to change the risk-benefit of tisotumab vedotin for any of these subgroups defined by prior therapy.
L1347: FDA agrees that no tisotumab vedotin dose modifications are needed in patients with mild hepatic impairment and in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. Tisotumab vedotin should be avoided in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. Please refer to Clinical Pharmacology section 6.3.2.3 for further information.

L1355: ## 8.2.8 Additional Safety Explorations
L1356: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1357: ## Data:
L1358: N/A
L1359: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1360: N/A
L1361: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1362: There were no human carcinogenicity studies of tisotumab vedotin and data are limited regarding long-term use.
L1363: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1364: ## Data:
L1365: N/A
L1366: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1367: There are no available human data on tisotumab vedotin use in pregnant women to inform a drug-associated risk of adverse developmental outcomes. Tisotumab vedotin can cause fetal harm based upon findings from animal studies of MMAEs. Female subjects of childbearing potential treated with tisotumab vedotin should be advised of the potential risk to the fetus.
L1368: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1369: FDA agrees that there were no pregnancies in the tisotumab vedotin development program. In the USPI, FDA advises that female patients of reproductive potential should have pregnancy status verified prior to initiating treatment with tisotumab vedotin. In addition, female patients of reproductive potential should use effective contraception during treatment and for 2 months after the last dose. Male patients with female partners of reproductive potential should use effective contraception during treatment and for 4 months after the last dose. Embryo-fetal toxicity is listed under Warnings and Precautions. Refer to Section 5.5.4 for additional information.
L1370: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1371: ## Data:
L1372: N/A
L1373: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1374: Tisotumab vedotin has not been assessed in children. Due to the effect of MMAE on microtubules, the treatment of children would be considered unethical. Tisotumab vedotin should not be given to children as it may affect their development. The FDA-agreed initial pediatric study plan (iPSP) received on 20 June 2019 includes a full waiver for the condition of cervical cancer in all pediatric age groups (birth to 16 years).
L1375: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1376: Although tisotumab had previously been granted a waiver for pediatric studies, it was
L1377: determined during this review cycle that tissue factor is a relevant target in pediatric tumors, and therefore, a new pediatric study plan, including a larger plan to investigate tisotumab in pediatric tumors, was needed. After discussion with the pediatric groups at FDA (CDER PERC Committee), it was determined that 2 post-marketing requirements would be put forth, requiring conduct of preclinical and clinical studies of tisotumab in pediatric tumors. These are described in section... In addition, an amended iPSR including a full waiver for pediatric studies for the cervical cancer indication, but a deferral for additional pediatric studies with tisotumab, based upon information from additional studies assessing tissue factor as a relevant molecular target in pediatric malignancies.
L1378: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1379: ## **Data:**
L1380: A single subject in pivotal trial GCT1015-04 received an overdose at 117% of the recommended 2.0 mg/kg dose of tisotumab vedotin (in cycle 1 and cycle 2). No TEAEs were experienced by this subject that were attributable to this accidental overdoses.
L1381: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1382: Neither the effects of overdose of tisotumab vedotin nor an antidote to overdose are known. Tisotumab vedotin is not likely a drug of abuse potential. The potential for tisotumab vedotin drug abuse and dependence is unknown.
L1383: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1384: FDA agrees that the Applicant's assessment.
L1385: ## 8.2.9 Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1386: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1387: ## **Data:**
L1388: N/A
L1389: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1390: Tisotumab vedotin is not currently approved or marketed in any country.
L1391: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1392: FDA agrees that tisotumab vedotin is not currently approved or marketed in any country.
L1393: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1394: ## **Data:**
L1395: In addition to the studies presented in this application, which includes ongoing pivotal trial GCT1015-04 and trial SGNTV-001 (ADA, ECG, and QTcF data), additional ongoing trials with tisotumab vedotin include the following:
L1396: - GCT1015-05, a Phase 1b/2 open-label trial of tisotumab vedotin (HuMax-TF-ADC) monotherapy and in combination with other agents in subjects with recurrent or stage 4b cervical cancer treated with 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W or [REDACTED](b)(4) This includes 142 subjects as of 16 Aug 2020 data cutoff.
L1397: - GCT1015-06, an open-label phase 1/2 trial of tisotumab vedotin in Japanese subjects with previously treated recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer (Part 2) or advanced solid malignancies (alveolar sarcoma, squamous esophageal, squamous cervical, ovarian, colon adenocarcinoma; Part 1). As of the data cutoff of 16 Aug 2020, 23 subjects had been treated with [REDACTED](b) (4) or 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, including 17 subjects in Part 2 and 6 subjects in Part 1).
- SGNTV-002, an open-label phase 2 study of tisotumab vedotin in patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. As of the data cutoff of 16 Aug 2020, 55 subjects have been treated with a safety run-in of a dose-dense regimen: 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, [REDACTED](b) (4)
- SGNTV-003, a randomized, open-label phase 3 trial of tisotumab vedotin (2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W) versus investigator's choice of chemotherapy in second- or third-line recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer.
L1398: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1399: All safety concerns that have been identified are adequately represented in the safety database for tisotumab vedotin. Ongoing clinical trials will contribute additional safety information to further elucidate the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin.
L1400: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1401: FDA agrees that the safety of tisotumab vedotin has been characterized in GCT1015-04 and other trials reviewed as part of this application. FDA agrees that additional ongoing trials will contribute additional safety information. FDA will also continue to monitor post-marketing reports and safety reports submitted after approval.
L1402: ## 8.2.10 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1403: ## **Data:**
L1404: The integrated safety profile of tisotumab vedotin in this application is based upon a total of 272 subjects with malignant tumors, primarily cervical cancer. The pivotal trial GCT1015-04 consists of 101 subjects with previously treated, recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer. All subjects received the recommended dose of 2.0 mg/kg of tisotumab vedotin 1Q3W. Overall exposure is adequate to support the characterization of the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin.
L1405: At 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W most AEs were manageable with either supportive or preventative care or dose modifications. The overall characteristics of the population studied in GCT1015-04 are representative of, and generalizable to, subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who have received at least one prior systemic therapy. AESIs for tisotumab vedotin are ocular AEs, epistaxis, and peripheral neuropathy. Of those, ocular AEs and peripheral neuropathy have been classified as important AESIs. Most ocular events were grade 1 or 2, were transient, and achieved resolution. The protocol-defined OMP measures are effective. With these measures, most events are low grade and resolve or improve quickly. The measures also reduced frequency of ocular events and led to fewer treatment discontinuations. Peripheral neuropathy AESIs were mostly grade 1 and 2 and were manageable with dose modifications and standard supportive care. Major bleeding events were typically associated with tumor location, were
L1406: manageable with standard supportive care (eg, transfusions), and did not require more aggressive measures such as use of clotting factors. There were no AEs of bleeding associated with fatal outcomes.
L1407: No new safety signals were identified.
L1408: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1409: Based on the safety results observed, tisotumab vedotin, at the recommended dose of 2.0 mg/kg 1Q3W, has a manageable and tolerable safety profile in subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who previously received chemotherapy doublet plus bevacizumab, if eligible.
L1410: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1411: FDA agrees with the Applicant that the safety database is adequate to characterize the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin.
L1412: In GCT1015-04, the most common (\ge 30\%) adverse reactions were hemorrhage, fatigue, nausea, peripheral neuropathy, alopecia, and conjunctival adverse reactions.
L1413: Serious adverse reactions occurred in 43% of patients and included ileus, hemorrhage, pneumonia, peripheral neuropathy, acute kidney injury, sepsis, constipation, and pyrexia. Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 4% of patients and included septic shock, pneumonitis, sudden death, and multisystem organ failure, each in 1 patient.
L1414: Treatment discontinuations due to TEAEs occurred in 13% of patients, most commonly due to peripheral neuropathy and corneal adverse reactions. Dose reductions occurred in 23% of patients, most commonly due to conjunctival adverse reactions and corneal adverse reactions.
L1415: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's identification and characterization of important AESIs. Ocular toxicity is an important safety signal for tisotumab vedotin and to alert providers to the potential for severe ocular toxicity such as development of corneal ulcers, FDA elevated ocular toxicity to a Boxed Warning in the USPI.
L1416: While FDA agrees that the OMP measures reduced the frequency of ocular toxicity, FDA notes that the OMP measures, particularly ophthalmic steroids, also have risk. Ophthalmic corticosteroids should be used consistent with current labeling which includes a slit lamp exam prior to the initial prescription and prior to all renewals.
L1417: FDA agrees that peripheral neuropathy is an important AESI and labeled this under Warnings and Precautions. In addition, FDA classified hemorrhage as an important AESI due to its frequency and the potential for severity and labeled it under Warnings and Precautions. Pneumonitis is an important safety signal associated with antibody-drug conjugates containing vedotin, and two patients in GCT1015-04 experienced pneumonitis, including one patient who died. FDA also included pneumonitis under Warnings and Precautions.
L1418: Overall, the safety profile of tisotumab vedotin is manageable through labeling and supportive oncologic care and acceptable for the indicated population which has a serious and life-threatening disease and limited therapeutic options.
L1419: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1420: ## 8.3 Statistical Issues
L1421: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1422: There were no major statistical issues with the study design, statistical analysis plan, or efficacy results of Study GCT1015-04. The study showed a confirmed IRC-assessed ORR of 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9%, 33.3%) in 101 subjects with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer, and the median DOR among the 24 confirmed responders was 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2 month, NR).
L1423: No inferential procedures were used to evaluate results from this single arm study. Instead, the efficacy evaluation was based on the magnitude of response rate and adequate duration of response. Additionally, although PFS and OS results were summarized, time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable without a comparator arm.
L1424: ## 8.4 Conclusions and Recommendations
L1425: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1426: In GCT1015-04, treatment with tisotumab vedotin resulted in an ORR of 23.8% (95% CI: 15.9, 33.3) and a median DOR of 8.3 months (95% CI: 4.2, NR) in patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer who had received \le 2 lines of systemic treatment in the recurrent or metastatic setting, including doublet chemotherapy, with bevacizumab if eligible. Available therapies in this treatment setting include single agent chemotherapies with ORRs ranging from 5%-21% and median DORs ranging from 2-4 months, but these data collected prior to establishment of a chemotherapy doublet with bevacizumab as standard-of-care first-line treatment for patients in the U.S. Given that all patients who received tisotumab vedotin on GCT1015-04 received first-line doublet chemotherapy and 69% of patients had prior bevacizumab as per U.S. standard of care, the review team considers that the efficacy of tisotumab vedotin demonstrates an improvement compared to available therapies and is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in the indicated population.
L1427: Tisotumab vedotin demonstrated acceptable safety and tolerability in the indicated patient population with a serious and life-threatening condition and limited therapeutic options. The safety of tisotumab vedotin is manageable through labeling, which includes a boxed warning for ocular toxicity, premedication, dose modification, and supportive care.
L1428: Based on a favorable benefit-risk assessment, FDA clinical and statistical reviewers recommend accelerated approval for the following indication:
L1429: *Tivdax is a tissue factor-directed antibody and microtubule inhibitor conjugate indicated for the treatment of adult patients with recurrent or metastatic cervical cancer with disease progression on or after chemotherapy.*
L1430: *This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and durability of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in confirmatory trials.*

L1559: ## 19.4.1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance

TV-001	PRA-US-LML-0356	PRA-US-LML-0357	PRA-US-SML-0378
L1563: ADC (tisotumab vedotin)
L1564: The bioanalytical methods for measuring tisotumab vedotin plasma concentrations are shown in Table 40. ELISA assays were used to measure tisotumab vedotin concentrations, which utilized TFECDHis as a capture reagent and an anti-MMAE antibody for detection. Briefly, TFECDHis was coated on an ELISA plate. Diluted calibrators, QCs, and study samples were transferred to the plate and the tisotumab vedotin present was allowed to bind to the coated TFECDHis. A mouse IgG2amm-Aur1F10 (anti-MMAE) detection antibody directed against the MMAE part of HuMax-TF-ADC, was added to bind to the conjugated tisotumab vedotin present. A horseradish peroxidase (HRP)-conjugated goat-anti-mouse IgG was added to bind the formed complexes, followed by ABTS substrate for color development.

L1572: ## Total antibody
L1573: The bioanalytical methods for measuring total antibody plasma concentrations are shown in Table 41. ELISA assays were used to measure total antibody concentrations, which utilized TFECDHis as a capture reagent and an anti-hIgG1 antibody for detection. Briefly, TFECDHis was coated on an ELISA plate. Diluted calibrators, QCs, and study samples were transferred to the plate and allowed to bind to the coated TFECDHis. A mouse anti-human IgG1-HRP detection antibody was added to the wells to detect the tisotumab and/or tisotumab vedotin present. ABTS substrate was subsequently added for color development.

L1592: ## 19.4.2 Pharmacometrics review
L1593: ## Review Summary
L1594: In general, the applicant's population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1595: More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 44.

L1600: ## Population PK Analyses
L1601: **Objectives:** 1) Develop population models that describe the pharmacokinetics (PK) of antibody-drug conjugate (ADC) and Monomethyl auristatin E (MMAE) following intravenous administration in patients with metastatic solid tumors; 2) Estimate population PK parameters of the models and estimate and summarize individual PK parameters; 3) Estimate the inter-individual variability of model parameters in
L1602: the patient population; 4) Estimate the residual (intra-individual) variability of ADC and MMAE concentrations in the patient population; 5) Identify intrinsic and extrinsic covariate factors that influence distribution and elimination of ADC and MMAE, and to estimate the effects of these covariates on patient's PK parameters and exposure estimates; 6) Determine whether presence of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) affects PK of tisotumab vedotin following IV administration in patients with metastatic solid tumors; and 7) Estimate individual values of ADC and MMAE exposure for characterization of covariate effects and for exposure-response analyses.
L1603: **Data:** The data include a total of 4847 ADC and 5145 MMAE concentration values from 399 patients enrolled in studies GEN701, GEN702, GCT1015-04 and SGNTV-001. The data cut for the ongoing studies (GCT1015-04 and SGNTV-001) was 22 Aug 2019. Some of the concentrations below the limit of quantification were included in the analysis using M3 method. Patients covariates are summarized overall and by study in Table 45 and Table 46. Note that there are 68.2% baseline HSC scores missing and the missing values were imputed with median of 120 per the data define file. This may reduce the power of investigating the relationship between baseline HSC and confirmed best overall response. In addition, only 6 black patients (1.5% of the overall study population) were enrolled in the studies. The studied population may not represent the real disease population.
